- **AWS Builder Center student rewards: free Skill Builder year + badge-earned credits, no credit card required** (high) — Verified students at accredited higher-education institutions (verified via SheerID with name, college name, and school email — .edu domain NOT strictly required) get: 1 year of AWS Skill Builder (900+ courses, labs, cert exam prep, game-based learning) just for completing a profile; badge-based rewards — 7 badges = $10 AWS credits, 14 badges = $20 AWS credits, 21 badges = $100 AWS Certification voucher; plus free workshops/courses/community, explicitly 'No credit card'. Student Builder Groups (student-led campus communities) also exist.
  - *Eligibility:* Students (or staff) of an accredited higher-education institution, verified by SheerID (most checks minutes; up to 24-48h with extra docs). No startup/funding rules.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in with an AWS Builder ID at builder.aws.com → complete SheerID student verification → complete profile (unlocks Skill Builder) → earn badges (articles, comments, streaks) to unlock the $10/$20 credits and $100 voucher. Credits apply to AWS usage (incl. messaging services) if you have/created an AWS account.
  - *Expiry:* Skill Builder benefit is 1 year; badge credits/voucher have no expiry stated on the page; program is ongoing.
  - *Gotchas:* Rewards are small ($10-$20 credits, $100 voucher) — meaningful but not a production budget; the cert voucher is a voucher, not exam free passage; no card needed but actual AWS production usage still requires a billing account.
  - *Source:* https://builder.aws.com/student-rewards
- **AWS Free Tier — Free Plan: up to $200 credits, 6 months, no charges** (high) — As of the July 15, 2025 revamp (current structure, verified today): new customers get $100 in credits immediately on sign-up and can earn up to $100 more (5 console activities worth $20 each: launch an EC2 instance, create an RDS database, build a Lambda web app, try a Bedrock foundation model, set an AWS Budgets cost budget). Free plan = access to 90+ select services for up to 6 months with no charges and no surprise overages; you cannot exceed limits — remaining credits auto-apply. Upgrade to Paid plan any time; on the old sign-up experience unused credits stay usable up to 12 months from sign-up, but under the new sign-up experience (launched Oct 2026) the FAQ states Free Tier credits expire 6 months from account creation. Free plan accounts auto-enroll at account creation.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers only (an existing or previously-had AWS account makes you ineligible). No .edu email or student status required — open to anyone worldwide. Credit card NOT required for most new customers (FAQ Q4: 'A payment method isn't required to sign up for most new customers. In some cases, we may request additional information, such as a payment method, to verify your identity'). NOT available in China (ZHY/Ningxia, BJS) regions or AWS GovCloud (US) regions (certain services such as Lambda excepted). Free plan + credits are NOT available through AWS Organizations: enrolling a Free plan account in Organizations upgrades it to Paid, and an Organization can benefit from offers via only one account (usage aggregated).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an AWS account, select Free plan (default). 2) $100 credit appears on the Credits page (up to 10 minutes to show). 3) Complete the five 'Explore AWS' activities in the AWS Management Console Home widget to earn up to $100 more. 4) Monitor balance/days remaining via the Cost & Usage widget or Settings Portal (new sign-up experience) / Credits page in Billing console (pre-Oct-2026 sign-ups). 5) Upgrade to Paid plan to continue after expiry.
  - *Expiry:* Free plan expires at the earlier of 6 months from account creation or credit exhaustion (cannot be extended). After expiry AWS suspends the account and resources; data is retained 90 days, then the account and all content are permanently erased unless upgraded within 90 days. Credits expire 6 months from account creation on the new sign-up experience (FAQ); 12 months per the July 2025 Free Tier Terms for earlier sign-ups.
  - *Gotchas:* Suspended after expiry — move data/projects before the 90-day window or lose them. Free plan is 'evaluation only' per terms (sensitive data processing disallowed). Credits can't be transferred to other accounts. AWS may reclaim resources after 3 months of inactivity with 30 days' notice. Unused monthly free amounts don't roll over. Activating paid-only services upgrades you to Paid plan (bills on pay-as-you-go beyond credits). EC2/RDS/S3/API Gateway on new accounts are no longer fixed monthly free allowances — they consume the $200 credit pool; new free-plan-eligible EC2 types: t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large (t2.micro is legacy only).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/free-tier-faqs/
- **AWS Free Tier — 30+ Always Free services (all customers, indefinite)** (high) — All AWS customers (new and existing, Free and Paid plans) get over 30 'Always Free' services with monthly usage caps; usage is aggregated across all regions; excess usage automatically applies Free Tier credits (Free plan) or bills at standard on-demand rates (Paid plan). Representative always-free caps (per service docs and community verification, not all published on the /free/ page itself): Lambda 1M requests + 400,000 GB-seconds; DynamoDB 25 GB + 25 provisioned RCU / 5 WCU; SQS 1M requests; SNS 1M publishes; CloudWatch 10 custom metrics + 10 alarms + 1M API requests; CloudFront 1 TB data transfer out.
  - *Eligibility:* Any AWS customer — no .edu, no student status, no credit card required for the account itself. Available in regions where AWS operates (Always Free offers are region-broader than the Free Plan credits, which exclude China and GovCloud).
  - *How to claim:* Automatic once you have an AWS account; no application.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while you hold an AWS account; monthly limits reset each month with no rollover. AWS may terminate any offer at any time.
  - *Gotchas:* Data transfer out (egress) beyond CloudFront's 1 TB and inter-region traffic are billable; paid add-on features auto-bill on Paid plans; individual service caps vary — check each service's pricing page before heavy use. The per-service figures above are medium-confidence (sourced from the EC2 docs and builder-community summaries, since the /free/ page lists service tiles without caps).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Free Tier — short-term trials on selected Paid-plan services** (medium) — Selected Paid-plan services can be 'tried' with limited free trials; the trial begins when you activate the service, and any eligible Free Tier credits automatically apply to usage beyond trial limits. Some offers are limited in duration or free-usage amount; on a Free plan, activating a paid-only service upgrades the account to Paid plan.
  - *Eligibility:* New AWS customers (per-service trials listed on aws.amazon.com/free/); no .edu required; same region caveats (Free Plan credits unavailable in China/GovCloud).
  - *How to claim:* Activate the trial service from the console; track the trial end date yourself — AWS does not always notify when a trial ends.
  - *Expiry:* Per-service trial windows; AWS may end any offer at any time.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs Always Free confusion: trial periods end and pay-as-you-go billing starts without a notification in some cases — delete trial resources before expiry. Egress/data-transfer and public IPv4 charges ($0.005/hr) commonly surprise trial users.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/free/
- **AWS Educate — free self-paced curriculum, hands-on labs, badges, job board** (high) — Free, self-paced online cloud training 'designed for beginners': hundreds of hours of content incl. Getting Started with Storage (S3), Compute (EC2), Networking (VPC), Databases (RDS), and Cloud Operations + cost-estimation labs, a Cloud 101 pathway, and a Cloud Literacy interactive badge. Includes free hands-on labs in a sandboxed AWS Console environment (time-boxed, unlimited retries), shareable Credly digital badges, an invitation to the AWS Emerging Talent Community, and an AWS Educate Job Board (18+) with in-demand jobs and internships. No dollar-denominated AWS cloud credits — it is a learning program, not a credit grant.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to any individual regardless of education, experience, or career journey — NO .edu email or university affiliation required. Register with just an email address; no credit card needed. Minimum age: 'as young as 13' per the current page; the Educate FAQ says 14+ generally, with EU 16+; Algeria, Lebanon, Portugal 18+; not offered in China or Switzerland.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Register at the AWS Educate sign-up page (promo code 'EducateLP' referenced on the page) or sign in to AWS Educate. 2) Choose a learning path (Cloud 101 recommended for new-to-cloud). 3) Complete labs/courses. 4) Claim digital badges on Credly. 5) Job board access at 18+.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; content and lab time limits update over time; lab attempts are time-boxed but retries are unlimited.
  - *Gotchas:* Labs run on shared sandboxes, not your own AWS account (does not stack with your Free Tier account). No monetary credits. Employer direct-messaging on the job board is US-only per the FAQ. Age minimum varies by country (higher in EU and some regions than the '13+' marketing). No certification exam included — AWS certifications remain paid (see AWS Academy vouchers).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/education/awseducate/
- **AWS Skillbuilder — 500-600+ free self-paced digital training courses** (high) — AWS's Training FAQ: 'Self-paced digital training on AWS Skill Builder is free.' Free digital training includes 500+ on-demand courses in 17 languages (the /training/ page markets '600+ free digital courses'); free digital badges (knowledge, Cloud Quest) earned at >=80% on the associated assessment, claimable on Credly. Paid Individual/Team subscriptions add more content and labs; free webinars/Twitch training events also exist, while classroom training and certification exams cost money.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone — no .edu or student status; sign in with a free AWS Builder ID (or APN credentials / company Team subscription if an org member). No credit card.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Open AWS Skill Builder and sign in or create a free AWS Builder ID. 2) Enroll in any free digital course. 3) Score >=80% on the assessment to earn a badge. 4) Claim the badge via Credly.
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry for free access; subscription content changes with plans.
  - *Gotchas:* Only part of Skillbuilder is free — advanced lab-heavy content is behind paid Individual/Team subscriptions; certification exams are paid (vouchers only via AWS Academy pathways); don't confuse free digital badges with paid AWS certification badges.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/faqs/
- **AWS Activate — Founders package: $1,000 credits (select participants up to $5,000)** (high) — For self-funded, early-stage startups new to AWS Activate Credits: $1,000 USD in AWS credits (per AWS's gaming-startups page: 'Start with $1,000 USD in Activate Credits. Select participants may qualify for additional credits up to $5,000'). Credits redeem on 200+ eligible AWS services incl. third-party foundation models on Amazon Bedrock. Also includes technical support, business mentorship, co-build/co-market/go-to-market resources, free pre-built cloud templates, AWS Startup Advisor, AWS Support Plans eligible for credits, and access to 80+ third-party offers. New customers also get the AWS Free Tier when creating a new account.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup program — NOT an education/.edu program (student-founded startups can apply as startups). Criteria: self-funded (or pre-Series B per AWS's June 2026 guide), founded in the past 10 years, a fully functioning company website or public company profile, new to Activate Credits (or requesting more than previously received), and a business email matching the company domain. No student-specific grant exists.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a personal AWS Builder ID with a professional email. 2) Complete the AWS Activate profile. 3) Choose the Founders package. 4) Submit company/product/funding details (accurate info required; inaccuracies can be denied). 5) Link an AWS account and submit; decision in 7-10 business days by email, trackable in the console.
  - *Expiry:* Credits are subject to the AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions (fixed validity from grant, not re-stated on these pages); not renewable at the same value without a larger request.
  - *Gotchas:* Without a functioning startup/website you won't qualify. AWS has final say; website professionalism is weighted heavily. Credits don't cover Marketplace third-party services, Route 53 domain registration/transfer, most support tiers, or Professional Services (except Bedrock third-party model charges). AWS offers generally not available in China regions.
  - *Source:* https://startups.aws.com/lp/aws-activate-credits
- **AWS Activate — Portfolio package: up to $200,000 credits (provider-backed)** (medium) — For startups affiliated with an AWS Activate Provider (accelerator, VC, incubator, or partner org — e.g., Y Combinator, a16z, nVIDIA, Carta): up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits per the main /startups/ page and AWS's June 4, 2026 step-by-step guide ('The Activate Portfolio tier offers up to $200,000 in AWS Activate Credits for provider-backed startups'); AI startups ready to scale can get additional credits beyond $200,000 via their Account Manager. Also: 80+ exclusive third-party offers/memberships valued up to $800,000, dedicated account managers, and solutions architects. Note: the /free/offers/ comparison page still lists the range as '$1,000 to $100,000' with eligibility 'funded up to and including Series A' — the $200k / pre-Series-B figures come from AWS's newer 2026 pages, indicating the cap and funding stage were raised in 2025-2026.
  - *Eligibility:* Must hold an Organizational ID from an AWS Activate Provider; pre-Series B (most recent funding round within the last 12 months, if applicable); founded in the past 10 years; a fully functioning company website; business email; must be new to Activate Credits or requesting more than previously received. Not open to direct application — provider affiliation is mandatory. No .edu/student path.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Enroll in a program with an AWS Activate Provider and get their Org ID/invitation. 2) Create an AWS Builder ID + complete the AWS Activate profile. 3) Choose the Portfolio package with your Org ID. 4) Submit details; link an AWS account; decision in 7-10 business days.
  - *Expiry:* Per the AWS Promotional Credit Terms & Conditions (fixed validity from grant).
  - *Gotchas:* Provider gatekeeping (AWS has final say and gets pickier above $5,000). Official AWS pages conflict today on the cap: $100k on /free/offers/ vs $200k on /startups/ and the June 2026 guide — treat 'up to $200,000' as the current advertised max and verify at application. Legacy sub-tiers ($5k/$10k/$25k/$100k; YC's up to $250k) are no longer publicly listed.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/
- **AWS Academy — free participation for accredited higher-education institutions (+ exam vouchers)** (high) — AWS Academy 'provides higher education institutions with a free, ready-to-teach cloud computing curriculum' (aligned to AWS certifications) plus Learner Labs. FAQ: 'There is no cost to join.' Educators may request a 100% discount voucher on AWS certification exam cost; member institutions may request a 50% discount exam voucher that students redeem at 100% of the voucher's value; students in badge-eligible courses earn Emerging Talent Community points redeemable for rewards such as discounted AWS certification exam vouchers. This is AWS's closest university-scoped free offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Institutional, not individual: accredited higher-education institution offering degrees, diplomas, certificates, or continuing-ed programs, with an authorized Program Administrator or Central Point of Contact (CPOC). No .edu-email-only rule, but institutional membership is required; individual students/educators can't self-enroll.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Institution applies to join AWS Academy (or is added to an existing membership). 2) CPOC nominates educators; educators complete the mandatory Getting Started course. 3) Educators teach courses; students use Learner Labs. 4) Educators/students claim exam discount vouchers per program rules.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while the institution remains a member; voucher/reward programs are 'subject to change' per the FAQ.
  - *Gotchas:* Vouchers discount exam fees only — they are not AWS usage credits. Institutional onboarding can be slow; the free value to students is mainly the curriculum, labs, and exam discounts — hands-on AWS usage still comes from the regular Free Tier (which needs no Academy affiliation).
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/training/awsacademy/faq/
- **2025–26 change:** SUNSET / MATERIAL CHANGES 2024-2026: (1) The classic AWS 12-month free tier (EC2 750 hrs/mo, S3 5 GB, 1M input + 1M output free Bedrock model-invocation tokens per month for 12 months across ~75 models, RDS 750 hrs, etc.) has been SUPERSEDED by the new Free plan / Paid plan model: $100 signup credits + up to $100 earned, 6-month window, 30+ 'always free' services, and notably no payment method required for most new customers. The Bedrock-specific free-token docs page (docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/free-trial.html) now renders empty/removed, and the new Free Tier FAQ contains zero mention of Bedrock free tokens, 750 EC2 hours, or 5 GB S3 - legacy per-service allowances no longer exist. So the 'free Bedrock tokens for students' front is effectively dead; Bedrock access now comes via (a) Free plan $100-200 credits, (b) AWS Activate credits, or (c) Kiro credits for agentic coding. (2) June 2024: AWS doubled the portfolio/accelerate credit ceiling from $100,000 to $200,000 (announced July 1, 2024, for startups that raised Series A in the past 12 months; seed stage stayed at $100,000 at the time; credits' expiry moved from 1 year to 3 years - per CNBC 2024-06-28). The current credits page (2026) shows one Portfolio tier at up to $200,000. (3) 2026: the credits page was redesigned (awslabs/startups PR #75, June 2026) renaming tiers to Activate Founders ($1,000 start, up to $5,000 - NOT the ~$100 sometimes misreported; the $100 figure matches the new Free plan signup credit, not Activate), Activate Portfolio (up to $200,000), and adding the invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier ($200,000+) plus the new Kiro Startup Credits program. (4) 'Known so far' figures from the task (~$100K Portfolio, ~$100 Founders) are outdated: Portfolio is now up to $200K and Founders is $1,000-to-start/up-to-$5,000. No standalone student Bedrock/LLM free offer from AWS exists today - the closest student fronts are the no-card Kiro Student Offer (1,000 credits/month x 12 months) and AWS Academy (training only, no credits). Verification caveat: today's live fetches of aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/ and free sub-pages intermittently returned 403 via proxy, but credits-page content was captured today via the official page mirror in the awslabs/startups repo and search snippets of the live page; free-plan figures came directly from aws.amazon.com/free/ and /free/free-tier-faqs/ read today.</sunset_notes>
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes (all verified on official AWS pages fetched today, 2026-10-22; WebSearch budget was exhausted so only official-page fetches were used): (1) The legacy usage-based 12-month AWS Free Tier (750 RDS hours, 5 GB S3, etc.) was replaced by a credit-based model for accounts created on/after July 15, 2025 — $100 at sign-up + up to $100 earned, Free plan (6-month auto-closing account) vs Paid plan (12-month credit validity); a refreshed sign-up experience launched October 2026, and pre-October accounts still see the old credits console. The RDS pricing page still documents the legacy 750-hour/20 GB free database for pre-July-15-2025 accounts only. (2) Per-service 'Always Free' and legacy free-trial figures (e.g., Athena GB-per-month, Redshift RA3/RA2 1-node 12-month developer tier and 30-day standard tier, Glue 1M source-records ETL free allowance, Bedrock character-based free tiers) no longer appear on today's service pricing pages — the credit model absorbed them, so do not quote legacy numbers for new accounts. (3) Activate program structure is now three tiers: Founders ($1,000, up to $5,000), Portfolio (up to $200,000 via provider Org ID), and an invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier adding $200,000+ for AI startups scaling past the Portfolio ceiling. Credits now explicitly cover Amazon Bedrock third-party models (OpenAI, Mistral, Anthropic, Meta). Note a discrepancy: /free/offers/ still lists Activate as '$1,000 to $100,000' — likely stale vs the $200,000 ceiling on the credits page/FAQ. (4) New Kiro startup credits program (up to a year of Kiro Pro+, 2/10/30-user tiers, 'limited time only', excludes France/Germany/Italy/Spain/Poland/UAE and ~13 other countries). (5) AWS Academy / Cloud Career Academies: no working page found on today's main AWS site (URLs 404; cloudcareeracademies.org DNS-failed) — status unconfirmed; AWS Educate (free, 13+, no credits/vouchers) is explicitly still active with a 2026 copyright. Also of note: AWS Educate bundles no cloud credits and no free exam vouchers on any page I could read today.
- **2025–26 change:** (1) AWS Free Tier revamp: the classic structure - 12 months of Free Trial on ~75 services plus a separate 'Always Free' tier - is no longer on the official page; it has been replaced (2025-2026) by the Free plan / Paid plan model with up to $200 in credits, 90+ services for up to 6 months, and '30+ services always free within monthly usage limits'. The exact launch date of the revamp could not be confirmed (web-search budget exhausted mid-session); the page read today (2026-10-22) shows only the new structure. (2) AWS Activate: the legacy 'standard program up to $1,000 in credits' copy no longer appears on the live portal; the join page now says 'Apply for up to $200,000 in Activate Credits' while the application widget caps at 'up to $100,000' - consistent with a headline increase beyond the old $100K Accelerate ceiling, unconfirmed via a dated press release. (3) AWS Impact Accelerator, the US-only predecessor (part of AWS's 2022 $30M commitment to Black, Women, Latino and LGBTQIA+ founders), was superseded by the global AWS Impact Bootcamps announced Jul 30, 2025; the 2022-2023 US cohorts are closed. (4) All AWS Impact Bootcamps application windows through the 2026 Tokyo and Berlin cohorts are closed (deadlines Feb 27 and Mar 13, 2026), so as of 2026-10-22 the $5,000 credits, $50K grant and $15K credits are only obtainable in future, not-yet-announced cohorts. (5) Research caveat: https://startups.aws.com/lp/aws-impact-bootcamps currently returns HTTP 500 at the server level (its Next.js content payload is intact and was extracted from the raw HTML); the shared WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted, so 'was this changed in 2025-2026' checks rely on today's live pages rather than dated announcements.
- **2025–26 change:** Key 2025-2026 changes: (1) 2025-07-15 cutover - AWS replaced the legacy 12-month service-level Free Tier with a credit-based model: new accounts get a 6-month 'Free plan' ($100 signup credits + up to $100 service-exploration credits = up to $200) or a Paid plan; accounts created before 2025-07-15 are grandfathered on the old 12-month per-service free tier (e.g. RDS: 750 hours/month single-AZ, 20 GB gp2 storage, 20 GB backup). (2) Free-plan accounts auto-close at 6 months or when credits run out; data retained 90 days, then permanently erased. (3) Startup program restructuring: the /startups/credits/ page now lists 'Activate Founders' (up to $5,000; starts at $1,000) and 'Activate Portfolio' (up to $200,000, was the $150,000 Founders' Program era ceiling; the previous 'Accelerate' up to $100,000 tier name no longer appears) plus two newer tracks - 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+, invite-only post-Portfolio) and 'Kiro Startup Credits'. The exact 2025-2026 rename/retirement dates could not be confirmed: WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted, so only the current official pages could be verified. (4) No .edu-gated student dollar-credit license was found: the education front is AWS Educate (free training/badges, no credits) and institutional 'special pricing' programs; AWS Academy pages returned 404 on fetched sub-paths, so its current status/offerings are unverified rather than confirmed active or sunsetting. (5) Free-plan credits are unavailable in China regions or AWS GovCloud (US); the Free plan also excludes all promotional credits (Activate), so startup credits effectively require a Paid-plan account.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) July 15, 2025 - the classic 12-month per-service Free Tier was replaced for NEW accounts with a credit-based model: $100 sign-up credit + up to $100 earned via 5 console activities, a 6-month Free plan window (account closes at 6 months or when credits run out), 90+ services, and 30+ always-free services; accounts created before July 15, 2025 retain the legacy 12-month tier. Free-tier-eligible EC2 instances changed too (t2.micro dropped; t3.small, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large added). (2) Short-term free trials are now paid-plan-only; the Free plan only gets 'Always Free' offers. (3) Feb 21, 2023 - AWS Educate stopped its old monetary grants ($35/$100 student grants, $75/$200 educator grants); educator tooling moved to AWS Academy. (4) Feb 16, 2026 - Free plan (Free Tier) accounts became ineligible for promotional credits, which affects AWS Cloud Credit for Research and similar programs (a Paid plan is required to hold awards). (5) Aug 20, 2026 - AWS launched Student Rewards on AWS Builder Center as part of a $500M+ student commitment; the old 'AWS Activate for Students' style offer no longer appears on aws.amazon.com - student benefits are now Builder Center Student Rewards + AWS Educate + AWS Academy. (6) October 2026 - new AWS sign-up experience launched; credit-expiry display moved to the Settings Portal (accounts that signed up before Oct 2026 still use the Credits page in the Billing console). No free offer listed above was fully sunset as of 2026-10-22.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) July 15, 2025 — AWS replaced the long-standing 12-month Free Tier (750 hrs/mo t2.micro/t3.micro + 30 GB EBS + 100 GB egress, 30+ always-free services) with a credit-based model for NEW accounts: $100 sign-up credits + up to $100 earned = up to $200, with a 6-month no-cost Free plan that auto-closes. Legacy 12-month benefits remain ONLY for accounts created before July 15, 2025. Also, since the revamp, a credit card/payment method is no longer required to sign up for most new customers (previously mandatory). A new sign-up experience (Settings Portal credit tracking) launched October 2026. (2) June 2026 — AWS Activate Portfolio credit ceiling doubled from $100,000 to $200,000; the Founders tier was standardized at $1,000 with an 'up to $5,000' extension for select participants; a new invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier (>$200K) was introduced (confirmed by the redesigned aws.amazon.com/startups/credits page © 2026 and the awslabs/startups repo content update June 16, 2026). Note: CNBC's June 28, 2024 report that Series A startups got $200K from July 1, 2024 was NOT reflected in 2025 official materials (which still said 'up to $100,000'); the cap only officially moved to $200K in June 2026. (3) April 2025 — AWS launched the $30M Impact Accelerator (new program, not a sunset). (4) No VPS/bare-metal/dedicated specific free offer was sunset, because none existed as a standalone front: bare-metal and dedicated hosts have never had free tiers and remain credit-eligible under Activate/Free Tier credits only. (5) GAIA 2025 cohort status and Impact Accelerator 2026 track openings could not be re-verified this session (shared web-search budget exhausted); confidence ratings above reflect that.
- **2025–26 change:** Sunset/changed in 2025-2026: (1) 15 Jul 2025 (announced AWS Summit NY, 15-16 Jul 2025): the 12-month Free Tier was retired for NEW accounts and replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 signup credit + up to $100 explore credits, 6-month auto-closing plan, 90-day post-expiry data retention); pre-cutoff accounts keep their legacy 12-month offers until each one's own expiry. (2) EC2 free-VPS offer sunset: t2.micro (and hours-based t3.micro 750 h/mo) free tiers no longer apply to new accounts; new free-tier-eligible EC2 types are t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large, consumed via credits. (3) AWS Activate was folded into AWS for Startups: /aws.amazon.com/activate/ now serves the startups program (startups.aws.com); headline credit figure is 'up to $200,000' with published tiers $1,000-$100,000, 80+ partner offers valued up to $800,000, plus undisclosed extra AI-startup credits and Kiro credits — replacing older multi-year $1M total caps and old student/faculty Activate tiers (up to $100k/$150k) which no longer appear on official pages. (4) The Free Plan explicitly cannot combine with any other promotional/incentive offer, and joining AWS Organizations/Control Tower auto-upgrades to Paid and voids remaining explore credits. (5) AWS signals a new sign-up experience in October 2026 (FAQ note); Free Tier Terms last updated 9 Jul 2025. Note: session web-search budget was exhausted, so 2025-2026 change verification relied on WebFetch of official AWS pages (free/, free/offers/, free/free-tier-faqs/, free/terms/, startups/, education/, EC2 docs, Lambda pricing, RDS free page, what's-new Jul 2025 entry, AWS blog 15 Jul 2025) rather than broader search.
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes (all verified against AWS pages today, 2026-10-22): (1) Jul 15, 2025 — the legacy "12 months free" tier was replaced for NEW accounts by a credit-based Free Tier: $100 sign-up credit + up to $100 earned, 6-month Free plan; every legacy 12-month offer on pre-Jul-15-2025 accounts (including legacy CloudWatch 12-month items) has now fully lapsed. (2) Oct 2026 — AWS launched a new sign-up experience: social sign-in (Google/GitHub/Apple/Amazon), no credit card needed for most customers, and credit tracking moved from the Billing console to a Settings Portal for accounts signed up after the rollout; the Free Tier FAQ now states base credits expire 6 months (vs. 12 months in the Jul 9, 2025 Terms) — a live contradiction AWS hasn't harmonized, so treat 6 months as the safe planning horizon. (3) Dec 3, 2025 — Kiro Startup Credits v1 launched (up to 100 users, pre-seed to Series B, apply by Dec 31, 2025) and closed; relaunched Apr 7, 2026 with 2/10/30-user tiers, early-stage to Series A (VC-backed per the current promotion), apply by Dec 31, 2026; startups with active AWS Activate credits are excluded from the Kiro promo. (4) Jun 2024 (context) — AWS doubled Portfolio credits $100K to $200K and extended expiry 1 year to 3 years; still the structure in effect. (5) Feb 2023 (context) — AWS Educate stopped issuing universal/institutional credit grants; it is now free learning only, and student credits now flow through Builder Center Student Rewards (up to $579 incl. $30 credits + $100 exam voucher, launched Aug 2026). (6) CloudFront's classic "100 GB/month always-free" line is now presented as a standing $0 "Free" plan tier with 100 GB + 1M requests + 5 GB S3 storage credits and no overage charges — not sunset, but restructured. No CloudWatch/observability offer was sunset; the known limits (10 metrics, 1M API requests, 5 GB logs, 100 canaries, 3 dashboards, 10 alarms, 100K X-Ray traces) all still apply as ALWAYS-FREE allowances. There is no CloudWatch-specific startup or student grant beyond the general AWS Free Tier credits and Activate credits, which can offset CloudWatch usage on a Paid plan.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes verified on official pages today (2026-10-22): (1) The legacy 12-month Free Tier is gone — the old 'free for 12 months' language no longer appears on aws.amazon.com/free; it has been replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 signup + up to $100 explore = up to $200 over 6 months), after which the account auto-closes (data kept 90 days, then erased). (2) The classic 'Always Free' list was narrowed in messaging to '30+ services' with monthly limits (legacy tier had 60+ services); per-service quota numbers are no longer published on the hub. (3) SageMaker notebook free allowance shrunk to 250 hours of sc.t3.medium for the first 2 months (vs legacy 750 hours/month ml.t3.medium for 12 months). (4) AWS Activate tiers renamed/revalued: 'Activate Community' no longer appears on the current credits page — it is now 'Activate Founders' (up to $5,000, start $1,000) alongside 'Activate Portfolio' (now up to $200,000, previously up to $100,000) plus a new invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+); a new Kiro Startup Credits track (up to 1 year of Kiro Pro+, 1/10/30 users) was added. The stale /free/offers page still shows old $1,000-$100,000 figures and 7-10 day processing, inconsistent with the current credits page ($5,000/$200,000, 5-10 days) — treat the credits page as authoritative. (5) WebSearch was unavailable during this run, so no third-party press confirmation of announcement dates was possible; all claims above are from the official AWS pages fetched today.
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Amazon Pinpoint sunset (HARD DATE): AWS ends support for Amazon Pinpoint on October 30, 2026 — console access and all Pinpoint resources (endpoints, segments, campaigns, journeys, analytics) terminate that day; only the messaging-channel APIs (SMS, voice, mobile push, OTP, phone number validate) continue under the rebranded "AWS End User Messaging" service. Anyone relying on Pinpoint journeys/campaigns must migrate before 2026-10-30. 2) Legacy per-service Free Tier replaced: the old model — e.g., "SES: 3,000 emails/month free for the first 12 months, then $0.10/1K" and "SNS: 100 SMS/month free, US regions" — is no longer published; as of the October 2026 sign-up experience, new accounts instead get up to $200 in Free Tier credits over 6 months (Free plan auto-closes at 6 months or when credits run out). The "then $0.10/1K" figure survives, but as the à-la-carte SES price, while NEW SES accounts default to the more expensive Essentials plan ($0.16/1K) starting July 21, 2026. 3) The formerly permanent "3,000 emails/month free (US regions, no purchase of sending capacity)" SES allowance is not listed on today's official pricing page — treat it as superseded by the credit model for new accounts; legacy account behavior not verifiable (docs pages didn't render). 4) The legacy 100-free-SMS/month US-regions tier also no longer appears on End User Messaging pricing; SMS is now fully pay-per-message. 5) AWS Activate credit ceiling was raised to $200,000 (some pages still show the old $100,000 figure — a 2025-2026 program change, not a sunset).
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes (verified against official AWS pages on 2026-10-22): (1) Classic 12-month Free Tier sunset for NEW accounts on July 15, 2025 — replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 + up to $100 earned = up to $200 over 6 months, auto-enrolled, no charges); accounts created before July 15, 2025 remain on legacy 12-month terms (750 hrs/mo t2/t3.micro etc.); legacy terms still published. (2) October 2026: AWS launched a new sign-up experience; the current FAQ now states Free Tier credits expire 6 months from account creation (vs 12 months under the July 2025 Free Tier Terms) and moved credit tracking to the Settings Portal — AWS's own FAQ is internally inconsistent between sections (6 vs 12 months), so treat credit expiry on the new experience as 6 months and verify per-account in the Settings Portal. (3) AWS Activate restructured in 2025-2026: old tiers (Starter $5k / Portfolio up to $100k / YC up to $250k / $5k-$100k sub-tiers) are no longer publicly listed; current structure is Founders ($1,000, up to $5,000 for select participants) + Portfolio (up to $200,000, cap raised from $100,000), with eligibility shifted from 'funded up to Series A' to 'pre-Series B, most recent funding round within the last 12 months', and the application flow now goes through the AWS Builder ID. AWS pages are inconsistent today: /free/offers/ still advertises '$1,000 to $100,000' while /startups/ and the June 4, 2026 AWS blog advertise 'up to $200,000'. (4) No education-only (.edu/student-email) free credits exist at AWS: no free offer requires a .edu or student email — AWS Educate is open to anyone 13/14+ with just an email, the Free Tier is open to any new customer, and AWS Marketplace 'education pricing' is a paid discount, not free. The only university-scoped free offer is AWS Academy (free institution participation + exam vouchers). (5) Free Plan/credits unavailable in China (ZHY, BJS) and GovCloud (US) regions, and not usable via AWS Organizations (auto-upgrades to Paid plan). (6) AWS Educate availability excludes China and Switzerland and raises minimum age in the EU (16) and Algeria/Lebanon/Portugal (18).
- **Critic correction** (AWS Activate (Amazon Bedrock)): Figures are out of date. The Activate Founders self-serve package is $1,000 (select participants can reach $5,000), not about $100. The Portfolio package, for startups tied to an Activate Provider with an Org ID, is now up to $200,000; it was $100,000 before June 2026. Credits can be used on Bedrock third-party models. AI startups ready to scale can talk to an account manager about more.
- **Critic correction** (AWS Startups): Range is outdated. Current tiers are Activate Founders at $1,000 (up to $5,000 for select startups) and Activate Portfolio at up to $200,000 (raised from $100,000 in June 2026). The top of the range should read $200,000 for Portfolio, not $100,000+.
- **Critic correction** (Up to ~$100K in AWS credits (Portfolio/Partner tier; ~$100 Founders self-serve)): The current official maximum is up to $200,000 for the Portfolio tier (requires a provider Org ID; pre-Series B). The Founders (self-funded) tier starts at $1,000, with up to $5,000 for select participants, not about $100.

### Baseten Startup Program

- **Baseten AI Startup Program ("Baseten for Startups") — up to $25,000 credits (Dedicated Inference/Training) + up to $2,500 (Model APIs)** (high) — Per-application grant, not automatic: 'Up to $25,000 in credits for Dedicated Inference or Training' plus 'Up to $2,500 in credits for Model APIs'. Credits are non-cash, applied to Baseten Inference and Training Services under Baseten T&C. Acceptance is at Baseten's sole discretion; the amount awarded is set per applicant. Extras with admission: support starting at response times 'within a few business hours', shared Slack channels and Zoom calls, cross-cloud autoscaling, GTM amplification for launches/raises, invitations to Baseten-hosted networking events. Page fetched live 2026-10-22; terms unchanged since the legal disclaimers' last update (October 7, 2025).
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage AI-first startups: 'artificial intelligence needs to be core to your primary products or solutions'; funding stage Seed to Series A; started less than 5 years ago; 'VC funded' framing on the page (criteria do not hard-require VC backing — bootstrapped AI startups appear possible but must confirm at application). Net-new customers only: never received any Baseten credits, and the company domain must not have been subscribed to a paid Baseten Service plan within 31 days of applying. Excluded: IPO'd or acquired companies, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content, dev shops, consultancies, agencies, crypto mining companies. No .edu requirement (students are not the target), no region limit stated. No credit card required to apply; no equity requested.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up for a Baseten account (login.baseten.co/sign-up). 2) Apply via baseten.co/startup-program/ → 'Join the program' → baseten.co/talk-to-us/startup-program/. 3) Wait for manual review (third-party trackers report ~2–10 business days; acceptance is discretionary). 4) On acceptance, credits are applied to your account. Do NOT burn any paid plan or use more than the default signup credits before applying — net-new status (no Baseten credits ever) and the 31-day paid-plan rule are the main disqualifiers.
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 6 months from grant date ('used within 6 months of their grant date and will expire if not consumed after 6 months'); no stated end date or renewal for the program itself.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Up to' amounts — award size is per-applicant, not guaranteed maximum. Net-new rule: receiving the default signup credits or a paid trial before applying can disqualify you. 31-day paid-plan rule (legal disclaimer #2, last updated 2025-10-07). Credits cover inference/training compute and Model API usage only; after the 6-month window unused credits vanish. Support SLA is 'business hours', not 24/7. JPMorgan's tracker lists tech support response at max 48h via dashboard UI. Common rejection reasons per trackers: personal email instead of company domain, thin/placeholder website, application data mismatched with Crunchbase/PitchBook.
  - *Source:* https://www.baseten.co/startup-program/
- **New-account signup ('starting') credits — unannounced amount, no application** (medium) — Official pricing FAQ (fetched 2026-10-22): 'Do you offer free credits to get started? Yes, new Baseten accounts come with credits so you can get to know the UI and experiment with deployments for free.' Baseten's billing docs (docs.baseten.co/organization/billing) add: 'New workspaces receive credits for testing and deployment. Baseten applies credits to the current invoice before charging a payment method; you do not need to redeem them.' No exact dollar amount is published by Baseten. Third-party trackers (pricepertoken.com, infrabase.ai, Perkstack, aicredits.dev — per their notes the figure traces to Baseten's changelog announcement of usage-based pricing) report the amount as $30 per new workspace; treat $30 as plausible but unconfirmed (the live balance in a new workspace's billing dashboard is authoritative). One-time, per-workspace grant; not recurring.
  - *Eligibility:* Any new Baseten account/workspace, no application, no funding/age/status requirement. No credit card required to receive the starting credits.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at app.baseten.co (email, Google, or GitHub), verify email, create a workspace. Credits are attached automatically — nothing to redeem. They offset all usage (dedicated GPU time, Model API calls, training) and are deducted before any payment method is charged; balance visible on the billing dashboard. If you don't see them, Baseten's docs say to contact them.
  - *Expiry:* No published expiry for the starting-credits balance; docs say 'Baseten doesn't offer a separate free tier or perpetual free plan' — beyond the starting credits you pay standard per-minute / per-token rates. When credits run out with no payment method on file, Baseten deactivates your models until one is added.
  - *Gotchas:* Exact amount is unannounced by Baseten (the $30 figure is third-party/changelog-sourced — verify in your billing dashboard). This signup credit counts toward 'never received any credits from Baseten before', so it affects Startup Program net-new eligibility — apply for the program before spending/using paid features. No idle-time billing (scale-to-zero supported). Model API rate limits on the Basic plan are defaults, not a free allowance.
  - *Source:* https://www.baseten.co/pricing/
- **Education / student offer — none exists (negative finding)** (high) — Baseten has no education license, .edu program, or student credits. Official support KB (support-legacy.baseten.co, article last updated ~1 month before 2026-10-22): 'We don't have a self-serve credits program for individual students or projects, so we can't grant credits from a support request — sorry to be the bearers. What does exist: Startups may qualify for the Baseten Startup Program.' The Startup Program's legal disclaimers explicitly exclude 'educational institutions'. Occasional one-off hackathon sponsorships (e.g., shared endpoints at HackMIT 2024, ad-hoc extra credits on request) are the only student-facing touches; none are standing offers.
  - *Eligibility:* Not applicable — no standing student/education offer. .edu email confers no benefits. Educational institutions are ineligible for the Startup Program.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim. If you are a student or academic, your only paths are the generic new-account starting credits, or forming an AI-first early-stage company to apply to the Startup Program.
  - *Source:* https://support-legacy.baseten.co/articles/7684075988-do-you-offer-credits-for-students-researchers-or-open-source-projects

### Camber

- **Camber Student plan via GitHub Student Developer Pack (monthly free compute)** (high) — Free Camber Student plan while enrolled: 40 CPU hours/month, 5 GPU hours/month, 50 GB storage, 50 agent messages/month. Runs on Camber's agentic AI data-science platform (connect Postgres/Trino/S3, build agents, run from Cursor or Claude Code via Camber MCP server). Verified verbatim on the official pack page today ([education.github.com/pack](https://education.github.com/pack)) and in the [SDP changelog](https://github.com/github-education-resources/Student-Developer-Pack-Current-Partners-FAQ/blob/main/SDP-changelog.md) entry of 2026-08-20. Claim: (1) verify in GitHub Education (school-issued email and/or dated proof of enrollment, 13+, enrolled in a degree/diploma program); (2) open [Camber's student pack page](https://www.cambercloud.com/github-student-pack); (3) click 'Sign-up with GitHub' (→ app.cambercloud.com/login/github) using the GitHub account that holds the verified pack; (4) confirm the Student plan quotas in the dashboard. Camber's own page repeats the offer but not the numbers — the numbers come from GitHub's pack page.
  - *Eligibility:* Students with an active, verified GitHub Student Developer Pack (school .edu email or official dated enrollment proof; GitHub age 13+). Global; no region limit stated. No credit card required to claim (third-party trackers confirm '$0 / none required'; free while enrolled).
  - *How to claim:* Verify GitHub Student Developer Pack → visit cambercloud.com/github-student-pack → 'Sign-up with GitHub' with the verified account → Student plan activates with the monthly quotas.
  - *Expiry:* Free only 'while you're enrolled'; lapses when GitHub Student status ends (GitHub re-verifies at enrollment boundaries; benefits drop off afterward and paying for the platform is optional). Monthly quotas reset each month.
  - *Gotchas:* No GPU on the public free tier — the 5 GPU hours are exclusive to the pack student plan. Quotas were reduced in the 2026-08-20 update (older third-party listings cite up to 200 CPU hours — those no longer apply). 50 agent messages/month is a hard cap on the Nova AI agent. The plan is genuinely free (not a trial) for the whole enrollment period, no card on file.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Camber public Free plan ($0, no credit card)** (high) — Public 'Free' plan at $0: 25 GB storage, community Slack support, AI and CPU usage included, NO GPU access. 'No credit card required.' Claim: 'Get started for free' / 'Try it Free' → app.cambercloud.com/login — open to anyone (not limited to students or startups). Read from the official [Camber pricing page](https://www.cambercloud.com/pricing) today.
  - *Eligibility:* Everyone, no enrollment or startup status required. Global. No credit card.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at app.cambercloud.com/login via the Free 'Get started' button.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing free tier; no expiry stated.
  - *Gotchas:* No GPU at all on this tier — if the goal is free GPU hours, only the student pack offer qualifies. 25 GB storage cap; GPU and heavy AI usage require a paid plan.
  - *Source:* https://www.cambercloud.com/pricing
- **Camber Pro / Teams 14-day free trials** (high) — Pro ($20/mo monthly; $18/mo annual billed $216/yr, 10% off) and Teams ($50/seat/mo; $45/seat/mo annual billed $540/seat) each come with a 14-day free trial. Trial requires a credit card, which 'will be billed at end of 14-day trial'. Pro/Teams include up to 4× L4 GPUs (Teams adds 200 GB storage, up to 4× L4s, 'generous' AI/CPU/GPU, 24h support). Enterprise is custom-priced (BYOC, BYOK, SSO; contact info@cambercloud.com).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone; card required; trial is time-boxed, not free forever. No student/startup-restricted trial found.
  - *How to claim:* 'Start your 14-day free trial' → app.cambercloud.com/login (from the pricing page).
  - *Expiry:* 14 days from trial start; card auto-billed at trial end unless cancelled.
  - *Gotchas:* This is trial-vs-free: credit card mandatory and you will be charged at day 14 unless you cancel. L4 GPUs, not H100-class — budget for small inference/finetuning workloads.
  - *Source:* https://www.cambercloud.com/pricing
- **Startup program / education license (separate from SDP)** (medium) — As of 2026-10-22 no Camber startup program, startup credits, or standalone education license exists — the only student pathway is the GitHub Student Developer Pack offer above; there is no funding-cap or company-age criterion because there is no such program. (WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted before I could do a dedicated 2025-2026 startup-program search, so a brand-new 'Camber for Startups' launched in the last days cannot be fully ruled out; official pricing page, student-pack page and docs site show none today.)
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — none found.
  - *How to claim:* n/a.
  - *Expiry:* n/a.
  - *Gotchas:* If Camber launches a startup program later it will likely be via Contact Sales (info@cambercloud.com).
  - *Source:* https://www.cambercloud.com/pricing

### Google Cloud

- **Google Cloud Free Trial — $300 welcome credit (90 days)** (high) — New customers get $300 free credit, valid 90 days from Free Trial billing account creation. Credit covers any product including overages beyond Free Tier limits; it does not roll over (expires 90 days from signup even if you upgrade to paid). Free Trial billing account auto-closes when the $300 is spent or 90 days pass unless you manually upgrade to a Paid billing account (then you keep remaining credit until the 90-day mark and keep Free Tier access). Eligibility: you must have NEVER been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and never signed up for the Free Trial before (one per person/billing profile). GPUs, Windows servers and some features are not covered. During trial you are not billed — only if you manually upgrade to paid do charges begin. 30-day data-export window after the trial ends. Compute Engine trial restricted to 8 concurrent cores.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, any country with GCP access (no .edu or startup status needed). Credit card/other payment method REQUIRED at signup for identity verification (Google may run a pending authorization, not a charge). US/Mexico government entities excluded.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at cloud.google.com (Get started for free) -> add card -> $300 credit is preloaded into the Free Trial billing account automatically; spend over 90 days on any product incl. Vertex AI/Gemini API; optionally click Upgrade in console to keep credit alive beyond trial features. Do NOT click 'Activate a paid account' unless you want charges.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from signup or until $300 is exhausted, whichever first; unused credit does not survive account closure. Free Tier usage continues if you convert to a paid account.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs Free Tier: the $300 is for trial billing only; Free Tier allowances are separate, not charged against it. CC required but you are only charged if you manually upgrade to paid. Egress, third-party models (e.g. non-Google LLMs on Vertex) cost above free allowances. 30-day migration window after end; GCE resources may be deleted and are not recoverable.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google Cloud Free Tier — 20+ always-free products (no expiry)** (high) — Per-product monthly free allowances, reset each month, do NOT expire but limits are 'subject to change'. Verified examples from cloud.google.com/free: Compute Engine 1 e2-micro/month; Cloud Storage 5 GB Standard; BigQuery 1 TB scanned/month; Cloud Run 2M requests + Cloud Run Functions 2M invocations/month; GKE 1 Autopilot/zonal cluster; Pub/Sub 10 GB messages; Vision AI 1,000 units; Speech-to-Text 60 min; Natural Language 5,000 units; Cloud Build 120 build-minutes/day; KMS Autokey 100 active key versions + 10k ops; Artifact Registry, Firestore 1 GB, Secret Manager 6 versions, Cloud Shell 5 GB. Includes 'AI APIs' free usage — the GCP-hosted AI endpoints (Vertex AI GenAI preview limits apply separately).
  - *Eligibility:* All new GCP users (not student-only). A Google Cloud billing account with a registered payment method is required even to use the Free Tier ('eligible customers' in a paid or trial billing account).
  - *How to claim:* Any GCP account (trial or paid) automatically gets the free monthly allowances; no separate claim. Sign up at cloud.google.com with a card.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry (renews monthly), but usage resets on the 1st; unused allowance does not roll over.
  - *Gotchas:* Overspending beyond free limits is billed pay-as-you-go to your card. Free Tier products are region/feature-specific (e.g., e2-micro, us-central1 historically); GPU workloads are not in the free tier. Third-party LLMs on Vertex AI are billed. 'Not charged against $300 credit' means free-tier usage is on top of trial credit.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Gemini API / AI Studio Free tier — free LLM access, no credit card** (medium) — The Gemini Developer API has a Free tier: free input/output tokens with limited access to certain models, plus free Google AI Studio usage; no billing account or card required (Free tier qualification = 'Active project or free trial'). Per the current official docs, exact RPM/TPM/RPD are now model-specific and only viewable in aistudio.google.com/rate-limit (docs stopped publishing the static table). As of mid-2025 community-referenced Free-tier limits were ~Gemini 2.5 Pro 5 RPM / 250K TPM / 100 RPD, 2.5 Flash 10/250K/250, 2.5 Flash-Lite 15/250K/1,000. Pay tiers: Tier 1 needs a linked billing account (cap $250/min-rolling), Tier 2 after $100 spent + 3 days ($2,000 cap), Tier 3 after $1,000 + 30 days ($20k-100k cap). Upgrading to paid now requires prepaying min $5. Flagship 'Gemini 4 Argon' is NOT available on the Free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a Google account; no card, no .edu. Region: available in most countries; Google AI Studio usage is free in all available countries.
  - *How to claim:* Go to aistudio.google.com -> create project -> generate an API key -> call Gemini models within Free-tier limits. For deploying up to 2 full-stack apps without GCP billing, use the Google Cloud 'Starter Tier' (AI Studio -> Deploy, eligible accounts only).
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite while the Free tier exists (no renewal); limits subject to change; RPD resets midnight Pacific.
  - *Gotchas:* Free-tier content/prompts may be used by Google to improve its products (Paid tier does not); newest flagship models excluded from Free tier; limits are per-project not per-key; from June 19, 2026 the Gemini API stops accepting requests from unrestricted API keys (must restrict keys to the Gemini API endpoint); '429' when hitting limits; AI Studio playground daily caps have been tightened in 2026 (community reports ~10-30 req/day for top models under capacity pressure).
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Start tier: up to $2,000 credits / 1 year (self-serve)** (high) — For digital-native tech startups with a working MVP planning to seek venture funding. Benefits verified from the official benefits page: up to $2,000 USD GCP credits valid for 1 year; $200 Google Skills training credits; Google Workspace Business Plus free 12 months (new signups); access to partner Startup Perks (e.g. Datadog, ElevenLabs 33M credits, Render $500, GitLab Ultimate 20 licenses) — the ones starred are Scale-only.
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 24 months; not yet received Google Cloud credits (beyond the free trial); tech startup that plans to raise VC funding; clear product roadmap. No funding cap (pre-funding/bootstrapped is fine). Decision at Google's discretion. Company email matching public domain expected; GCP account + billing ID needed.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the Google for Startups Cloud Program application form at cloud.google.com/startup (self-serve 'Apply now') — or via the Startups Perks/partner pages. Complete the onboarding checklist at cloud.google.com/startup/onboarding (billing account setup) so credits attach to the correct billing account.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 1 year from issuance. Program itself is ongoing (self-serve available).
  - *Gotchas:* Requires a GCP billing account (payment method on file; credits offset charges, card isn't used if spend <= credit). Third-party (non-Google) models on Vertex are billed directly, not covered. 'Beyond the free trial' means the $300 trial doesn't disqualify you.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Scale tier: up to $200,000 credits over 2 years ($350,000 for AI-first startups)** (high) — Year 1: 100% of GCP spend up to $100,000 in credits (AI-first startups: up to $250,000). Year 2: 20% of GCP spend up to an additional $100,000 (both tracks). Total: up to $200,000, or up to $350,000 for AI-first startups using/planning to use Gemini as a foundation of their product. Credits cover Google models (Gemini, Gemma); third-party models billed separately. Also: up to $12,000 Enhanced Support credits (1 year, 1-hr critical response), $500 Google Skills credits, $600/month Google Maps Platform usage credits (separate application), Workspace Business Plus free 12 months, dedicated Startup Success Manager + Customer Engineers, and starred Scale-only partner perks (ElevenLabs 33M, Datadog up to $100k, OpenSearch $100k+30k, Render up to $100k, etc.).
  - *Eligibility:* VC-funded (equity funding Pre-Seed/Seed within last 5 years; Series A within last 12 months); founded within the last 5 years; not yet received more than $5,000 in GCP credits; stealth mode OK with funding verification. AI track additionally requires Gemini (or planned Gemini use) as foundation of primary product. No explicit revenue cap; decision at Google's discretion.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at cloud.google.com/startup ('Apply now') or apply through a Google Cloud partner (VCs/accelerators: Y Combinator — dedicated GPU/TPU access + Scale-tier benefits for AI cohorts; 500 Startups, AI2, ERA, StartX, Berkeley Skydeck). Approval is reviewed; YC and similar partners get high acceptance rates.
  - *Expiry:* Credits structured as Year-1 match + Year-2 20% top-up, 2-year program window; no renewal beyond that (case-by-case requests exist via partners).
  - *Gotchas:* To hit the $100k Year-2 top-up you must actually spend $500k in GCP in Year 2 — it's a match, not a grant. Year-1 '100%' also means real spend must occur. Third-party LLMs excluded from credit coverage. GPU/TPU access in partner cohorts may be ad-hoc scheduling, not reserved capacity. Maps and some perks need separate applications.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/ai
- **Google Cloud for Education — student & faculty course credits (no credit card)** (medium) — Faculty at regionally-accredited US institutions (or degree-awarding universities in eligible countries; for-profit institutions excluded) apply via a form and receive GCP credit coupons: faculty get their own, and a URL is emailed that students open to verify student status (school/college email confirmation) and receive coupon codes. Credits land in per-course Cloud Billing accounts. Official help docs state the program 'does not require a credit card to sign up'. Credits valid for 12 months after the course start date / 12 months after redemption; Free Tier usage does not count against them; GCP credits may not be used for crypto mining; GPU access must be requested. Separate 'training credits': current students can request 200 free Google Skills credits (online labs, courses, badges; credits expire 1 year after issue; request processing up to 2 weeks).
  - *Eligibility:* .edu / school email of an eligible institution (regionally accredited in US; degree-granting in eligible countries abroad). Students get credits only through a course whose faculty applied. No funding or startup criteria. Amount per course varies (historically ~$50+ per course; Google awards at its discretion).
  - *How to claim:* Faculty: apply via the Google Cloud for Education form (cloud.google.com/edu). Students: use the invitation URL your faculty sends -> confirm school email -> redeem coupon code in the Google Cloud console (Billing -> Coupons). For Google Skills: request the 200 training credits via the training-credits form linked from cloud.google.com/edu/students.
  - *Expiry:* GCP course credits: valid 12 months from course start/redemption; per-course billing accounts. Google Skills credits: expire 1 year after issued.
  - *Gotchas:* Student credits are tied to faculty applications — there is no longer a self-serve $300/12-month 'student credit' grant on the official pages (the older student program appears sunset; see sunset notes). Credits are non-commercial, for learning. GPU/CPU/project quotas capped; requests may be denied. School email must be verifiable; .edu generators violate ToS.
  - *Source:* https://support.google.com/google-cloud-higher-ed/answer/10324788
- **Google Cloud Free Trial / program status as of 2026-10-22** (high) — No sunset: the $300/90-day Free Trial and the Free Tier remain current as of this date (pages updated 2025-2026, terms archived 2025-01-30). No new standalone free LLM 'trial credit' has been announced in 2025-2026; the AI offer is the Gemini API Free tier (no card) plus free-tier products. Startup program remains two tiers (Start/Scale) with the 2024 restructured amounts. Gemini API added spend-based tier caps and (in 2026) mandatory API-key restriction and Prepay min $5 for paid tiers.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a (status note)
  - *How to claim:* n/a
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* Treat static free-tier RPM/RPD numbers as volatile — Google moved them to a per-account dashboard in 2026.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/archive/terms/free-trial-20250130
- **Google Cloud Free Tier (always-free monthly quotas, 20+ products)** (high) — Free-forever monthly usage on select GCP products, e.g.: Compute Engine 1 e2-micro VM/month (us-west1/us-central1/us-east1; time-based across those regions) + 30GB standard persistent disk + 1GB egress; Cloud Storage 5GB US regional storage, 5,000 Class A / 50,000 Class B ops, 100GB NA egress; Cloud Run 2M requests, 360,000 GB-s memory & 180,000 vCPU-s, 1GB NA egress; Cloud Run functions 2M invocations; BigQuery 1TiB queries + 10GiB storage/month; App Engine Standard 28 F1-h/day + 9 B1-h/day + 1GB egress/day; Firestore 1GiB, 50k reads/20k writes/20k deletes per day; GKE 1 cluster charge/month; GCS egress and other egress outside NA or beyond limits bill at standard rates. GPUs/TPUs never free.
  - *Eligibility:* Requires an active GCP billing account (Paid or Free Trial) in good standing; no negotiated pricing contract. No .edu or age requirement; effectively free tier is available to any GCP account holder. Free-tier benefits on Cloud Storage apply only to us-east1/us-west1/us-central1.
  - *How to claim:* Create any GCP billing account (usually via the $300 Free Trial signup, which requires a card); stay within per-product monthly quotas and you are never billed. Do not create paid resources beyond quotas, or overage bills on your card.
  - *Expiry:* No end date, but Google reserves the right to change or eliminate limits with 30 days' advance notice; quotas do not roll over.
  - *Gotchas:* Common trap: creating a GCP account for 'free tier' still typically needs a card (Free Trial signup requires one); egress, cross-region traffic, GPU/TPU, and overage are billed; limits are per billing account.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Start tier ($2,000 GCP credits)** (high) — For 'digital-native' startups with a working MVP and plans to seek venture funding soon: $2,000 in GCP credits, valid 12 months (applies to GCP services and select GCP offerings; covers Google's proprietary models — program credits do NOT cover third-party models, which are billed directly). Also included: free-tier AI Studio (Generative AI Studio) access, 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free (new signups), $200 Google Skills training credit, and 'Startup Perks' (partner discounts/offers).
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 24 months; has NOT yet received Google Cloud credits beyond the free trial; digital-native tech startup with working MVP + clear business model + plans to seek VC funding soon. Excluded: service agencies, consultancies, traditional small businesses; companies that have IPO'd or been acquired; educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content companies, dev shops, crypto mining companies, token distributors contrary to regulatory guidance. Acceptance at Google Cloud's discretion. No VC funding required at this tier (it's the pre-funded stage).
  - *How to claim:* Apply self-serve at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (per-tier page: cloud.google.com/startup/pre-seed). You must sign in with a Google account whose business email matches your public website domain, and provide your 18-character GCP billing account ID (create a GCP billing account if you don't have one); Google reviews (most decisions within a few business days); credits are deposited to your billing account.
  - *Expiry:* GCP credits: 12 months from issuance. Workspace Business Plus: 12 months. Google Skills $200 credit: training credits; expiration not stated on the Start page (student program credits expire 1 year after issuance — treat similarly). No renewal; re-applications case-by-case via cloudstartupsupport@google.com.
  - *Gotchas:* Start-tier page does not state a credit-card requirement, but you need an existing GCP billing account to receive credits, and creating a new GCP account/payment setup typically requires a payment method (Free Trial signup definitely does, per docs). Workspace benefit: your company domain must not have been on a paid Google Workspace plan within 31 days of applying, and the benefit cannot be applied to annual Workspace plans. Credits excluded from Marketplace/third-party products; egress/networking costs beyond free tiers still billable.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/pre-seed
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Scale tier (up to $200,000 GCP credits over 2 years; $350,000 for AI-first)** (high) — VC-funded early-stage startups get up to $200,000 in GCP credits over two years: Year 1 = 100% credit up to $100,000; Year 2 = 20% credit on year-2 spend, up to an additional $100,000. AI-first startups: Year 1 = 100% up to $250,000, Year 2 = 20% up to +$100,000 → up to $350,000 total (equivalently, 'qualifying venture-backed startups can tap an additional $150,000 with the AI startup program' on top of the $200K base). Program credits cover Google's models (Gemini, Gemma); third-party models billed directly and not covered. Also: up to $12,000 Enhanced Support credits for one year (1-hour critical response), 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free, $600/month Google Maps Platform credits for one year (separate application), $500 Google Skills training credits, dedicated Startup Success Manager + Startup Customer Engineers, Web3 program access, and partner perks.
  - *Eligibility:* Must have received 'recent startup equity funding' (pre-seed to Series A by an institutional investor/VC, or common Web3 funding sources — SAFE agreements and verifiable token raises/blockchain foundation grants count; definition: Pre-Seed/Seed rounds within the last 5 years, Series A rounds within the last 12 months). Founded within the last 5 years (stealth mode OK with investor-verified funding). Must not have received more than $5,000 in GCP credits already. Not eligible: IPO'd/acquired companies, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content, dev shops, consultancies/agencies, crypto-mining companies, token distributors against regulatory guidance; private-equity, government-innovation-grant, crowdfunding, angel and friends-&-family funding do NOT qualify for Scale tier (may be evaluated for Start tier instead).
  - *How to claim:* Apply self-serve at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (per-tier page: cloud.google.com/startup/seed-series-a). Sign in with the company business email matching your public website domain + provide the 18-character GCP billing account ID + verification of funding (public sources; stealth: work with your investor). Decision usually within a few business days (manual review may take longer); credits deposit to your billing account and you receive an in-console benefit tracker.
  - *Expiry:* Year-1 credits usable in year 1; year-2 credits (20% of spend, capped +$100K) accrue in year 2 → program spans 2 years. Workspace 12 months; Maps $600/month for 1 year (separate app); Enhanced Support credits valid 1 year.
  - *Gotchas:* Year-2 benefit is a 20% discount-style credit on actual year-2 spend (max $100K) — it is not a pre-funded $100K; you must keep spending. Credits exclude Marketplace third-party offerings, third-party AI models, and per footnote only 'GCP Services and Select GCP offerings'. Workspace 31-day paid-plan rule applies. Google 'unable to accept' a long exclusion list (see eligibility); acceptance at Google's discretion. AI tier requires being genuinely 'AI-first' (AI in foundational structure/strategy, not a bolt-on) — some applicants get rejected even if they say AI. Data/egress: credits offset compute/AI usage, but egress and overage still bill if credits exhaust.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/seed-series-a
- **Startup program add-ons announced 2025–2026: 'New: AI benefits' ($350K ceiling), Web3 benefits, ISV Startup Springboard, Gemini Startup Forum** (medium) — The benefits page now labels 'New: AI benefits' (AI webinars/content, dedicated AI training, resources, experts, access to up to $350,000 in credits) and 'New: Web3 benefits' (Web3 Perks: partner offers from Quicknode, TRM, Allium and more). FAQ: Web3 startups get 'up to $200,000 USD over two years for Google Cloud and Firebase usage' plus Discord channel, foundation grants, VIP event access. Scale-tier page adds ISV Startup Springboard (12-week program for emerging AI startups: bespoke technical guidance + GTM resources) and the Gemini Startup Forum (Google for Startups + DeepMind + Cloud; curated in-person gatherings for seed–Series A founders). Benefits page also lists a 'Future of AI: Perspectives on generative media for startups' report and Cloud Startup Customer Engineers with faster response times for critical issues.
  - *Eligibility:* AI tier: Scale-tier VC-backed startups that are AI-first (AI foundational to product/strategy). Web3: startups with verifiable token raises / blockchain foundation grants (SAFE also eligible). ISV Springboard: emerging AI startups in the program. Forum: seed-to-Series A founders (invitation/curated).
  - *How to claim:* No separate application — benefits come with Scale-tier approval; Web3 program has its own qualification via the same application (flag yourself as Web3); Springboard/Forum through program onboarding. Re-applications or questions: cloudstartupsupport@google.com.
  - *Expiry:* AI-credits structure (Y1 $250K 100% + Y2 20% up to $100K) spans 2 years; Web3 $200K over 2 years; Springboard is a 12-week cohort.
  - *Gotchas:* Web3 'up to $200,000' covers Google Cloud AND Firebase (distinct from the $350K AI cloud-credits ceiling — these are different track framings). Third-party models still billed direct. AI-first qualification is subjective and at Google's discretion; being an 'AI company' in name isn't enough (FAQ example: another AI startup founder did not qualify).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/faq
- **Google Cloud for Education — faculty teaching credits (up to $100 per teaching staff + up to $50 per student)** (high) — Eligible faculty can apply for GCP credits for classroom use: up to $100 in GCP credits per teaching staff and up to $50 in GCP credits per student, so students get hands-on cloud experience at no cost. Application is limited to faculty (students cannot apply for their own teaching credits; they can encourage faculty to apply for their next course). Course start date must be no more than 1 year out; approved credits are redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after the course start date, and are good for up to 1 year after course start. Credits attach to specified school-issued email domains and are non-transferable. Faculty Community access is restricted to verified/approved faculty. Also linked: Career Launchpad program (coach students through Google Cloud certificates/credentials) and Google DeepMind AI Research Foundations curriculum.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only, at an academic institution; requires official school email with a valid .edu (or equivalent institutional) domain ('Please use your official school email address with a valid edu domain'; if none, you may nominate another address, credits attach to it and are non-transferable); must provide faculty directory profile link. Country restriction: the teaching program is 'currently only available in' a listed set of ~60 countries (Argentina, Australia, Canada, Germany, India, Japan, Nigeria, UK, US, etc. — the country dropdown on the form; if your country is absent you can only 'express interest').
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching/ (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty → 'Apply for Cloud credits'). Fill personal info with school .edu email, country, institution, faculty directory link, course details (name, start date, abstract, course URL) and member info (all school-issued domains, expected students/TA-staff count). Check spam for CloudEduGrants@google.com confirmations; redeem credits via the education-credits redemption guide once approved.
  - *Expiry:* Credits good for up to 1 year after course start; redeemable window = approval through 16 weeks after course start; course start must be within 1 year of application.
  - *Gotchas:* Not open to students directly (students must go through faculty). .edu/institutional email effectively required (nomination of alternate email loses the .edu gate but credits stay non-transferable). Country list is restrictive — not all countries served. Small amounts ($100/$50) — intended for learning, not production. Students should avoid accidentally signing up for the GCP Free Trial with personal cards in classroom contexts (Google's own faculty guide warns of this).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Google Cloud for Education — student training credits ($200 Google Skills credits) and free labs/badges** (high) — Students can request $200 in Google Skills (formerly Cloud Skills Boost) training credits, which expire one year after issuance, plus free access to online hands-on labs, skill badges, and courses; Google Skills Arcade (gamified challenges with badges) is free. The student page also points to DSC (Developer Student Clubs) and the Career Launchpad pathway. This is training credit, not GCP usage credit — students do not get a separate GCP-usage student credit program (the individual $300 Free Trial is the only personal usage credit, and it requires a card).
  - *Eligibility:* Students (individuals); request via the student training-credits form (services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudskillsbooststudenttrainingcreditsapplication/). No .edu gate stated on the page, but it's a student-facing learning program; free labs/courses also available without the credit.
  - *How to claim:* On cloud.google.com/edu/students click 'Request credits' → complete the Google Skills Boost student training-credits application form; $200 credits are issued to your account and usable on Google Skills courses/badges within 1 year.
  - *Expiry:* $200 Google Skills credits expire one year after issuance; free labs/Arcade have no stated end.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse with GCP usage credits — this buys courses/badges, not compute. A separate 'apply for free Google Cloud credits' line on the /edu hub for students appears to funnel to the same faculty/teaching-credit program (per the hub, students get free credits via the higher-ed program, i.e., via faculty).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Cloud for Education — research credits ('Academic Research Grants', up to $5,000)** (high) — Researchers can apply for up to $5,000 in GCP credits to advance research (computing power; GCP tools; proposal-based). Eligible roles: Faculty, Postdoctoral Researcher, PhD Student (and IT Admin option on the form), from accredited higher-education institutions or eligible nonprofit research institutions. Application requires: email (institution account preferred), country (restricted list of ~60 countries — program 'currently only available in' those countries), job title, organization (select from institution list), researcher/faculty directory link, project name, start date, a ≤250-word proposal (research problem, GCP tools, timeline, milestones, how GCP supports future work; Google Doc link allowed), field of research, intended use (proof of concept / evaluation-comparison / workshop-training / open-source development), optional Google Scholar link.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty / PhD students / postdocs at accredited higher-ed or eligible nonprofit research institutions in the listed approved countries; institutional email preferred; faculty directory link required. Related institutional program: GPAR (Google Public Sector — Program for Accelerated Research) with co-investment, industry-leading pricing for AI accelerators (GPUs/TPUs), early access to TPUs/foundation models/datasets (AlphaFold, Earth AI), agentic AI (Co-scientist, AlphaEvolve), compliance (HIPAA, CMMC 2.0, FedRAMP), on-campus research days — GPAR is currently US-only.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/ (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/researchers 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'). Submit the Academic Research Grant form with the fields above; credits are awarded to the institutional account and redeemable per the education-credits redemption guide.
