Funded tiers = Scale and Scale AI, $200K–$350K total credits, for startups with verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding (founded within the last 3–5 years). Footnoted math: AI startups get 100% of GCP spend up to $250,000 in Year 1 and 20% (matching) up to an additional $100,000 in Year 2 (max $350K); non-AI startups get 100% up to $100,000 Year 1 and 20% up to $100,000 Year 2. Perks also include credits for frontier models like Gemini and Gemma (third-party models in Model Garden/Marketplace billed directly), Google Skills training, Startup Success Managers + Customer Engineering, and 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus at no cost (separate application; company domain must not have had a paid Workspace plan within 31 days before applying; a separate Maps application is required for monthly Google Maps Platform credits). Credits cover most Google-owned pay-as-you-go GCP products; subscription/prepay products and 3P Marketplace/Model Garden items are excluded. Acceptance is discretionary (funding stage/type, business model, founder experience, product usage, vertical, age, growth); response in 5–7 days. Non-VC funding (private equity, bank loans, government programs, grants, crowdfunding, friends & family) does not qualify for the funded tiers; re-application is allowed if prior credits went unused and current-year criteria are met.
  - *Eligibility:* For-profit, digital-native company; not IPO'd/acquired; excludes educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content, dev shops, consultancies, agencies, crypto-mining companies, and token distributors in violation of local regulation; must operate in a region where GCP is GA and self-service. Funded tiers: verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding from a known investor, founded within the last 3–5 years. Requirements: GCP billing account ID (18 chars), business email whose domain matches the company website domain, active website (heavily AI-generated content screened and may be rejected). The application itself is free and needs no credit card, but a GCP billing account — which normally carries a payment method — is the delivery vehicle for the credits. Credits are non-transferable and cannot be shared, split, or resold; doing so terminates enrollment.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Prepare the 18-character GCP billing account ID, a business email whose domain matches the company website domain, and a live, non-placeholder company website. 2) Apply at cloud.google.com/startups ('Apply now'), pick tier (Start $2k, or Scale/Scale AI if funded; stealth founders via investor/VC referral). 3) Wait 5–7 days for the automated accept/reject email (check spam). 4) Credits deposit into the selected billing account; request the Workspace and Maps benefits separately.
  - *Expiry:* Credits granted over 2 years: Year 1 100% (up to $250K AI / $100K other), Year 2 20% matching up to an additional $100K; Workspace benefit = 12 months; program qualifications change annually and previous enrollment does not guarantee re-enrollment.
  - *Gotchas:* Funded tiers require VC/known-investor funding — non-venture money disqualifies you from Scale; company email domain must equal the website domain or extra proof is requested; third-party models and subscription-billed products are excluded; past GCP credit usage is a (discretionary) acceptance factor; don't expect the generic $300 trial to auto-upgrade into this program — it is a separate application; AI-generated-heavy websites are screened and can cause rejection.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startups
- **Google for Education faculty ('teaching') credits: up to $100 per teaching staff + up to $50 per student, for classroom use** (high) — Faculty of accredited institutions can apply for GCP credits for themselves and students so students get hands-on classroom experience. Verified numbers from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty: up to $100 per teaching staff member and up to $50 per student in Cloud credits. Terms (from the official application form, read today): up to 3 courses per application; course start date no later than 1 year from application; credits redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after the course start date, and valid up to 1 year after the course start date; confirmation emails come from CloudEduGrants@google.com (whitelist that alias); group projects can pool students' individual credits. Related: 'Career Launchpad Program' gives students access to Google Cloud Certificates/credentials content.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only (students should have their faculty apply) at an accredited institution; official school-issued email with a valid .edu/.ac domain required (a nominated alternate email is possible — credits attach to it and are non-transferable); available only in the listed ~65 countries (incl. US, UK, EU, India, Japan, Brazil, others). No credit card required — these are promotional education credits, distinct from the paid-card $300 trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Faculty complete the form at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty). 2) Provide the official school email, faculty directory link, institution + department. 3) Per course (up to 3): course name, expected start date, abstract + how credits will be used, a URL that proves you teach the course, student email domain(s), expected student/TA counts. 4) Submit; only the most recent submission is reviewed; on approval, credits attach to the institution's email domains.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid up to 1 year after the course start date; must be redeemed by 16 weeks after course start (from approval); non-transferable, educational use only.
  - *Gotchas:* Google's own faculty docs explicitly warn: keep students off the $300 Free Trial (it requires a credit card) — use these course credits instead; country list is closed; resubmitting a form replaces the previous submission (the most recent is the one reviewed); multiple sections of the same course in the same period count as one course.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/facultyandhttps://edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching/
- **Google for Education research credits ('Academic Research Grants'): up to $5,000 in GCP credits for 1 year; plus GPAR collaboration program** (high) — For accredited higher-education and eligible nonprofit research institutions: up to $5,000 in Google Cloud credits (cloud.google.com/edu/researchers says 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'). Terms from the official application form (read today): credit must be activated within 60 days of the project start date; it expires 365 days from the coupon redemption date or when the amount is fully used; non-transferable, no commercial value or commercial use; faculty accept on the institution's behalf, PhD students only for the described project. Related: GPAR (Google Public Sector — Program for Accelerated Research) gives expert/researcher collaboration from Google + partners and on-campus research days (a services program, not a credit pool).
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty, PhD students, and postdoctoral researchers from accredited higher-education institutions or eligible nonprofit research institutions, in the approved-country list (~65 countries; undergrad/master students are NOT eligible for research grants). Institution email preferred. No credit card required for the credits themselves (a GCP billing account ID is still needed to receive them).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Fill the form at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/researchers). 2) Provide job title (faculty/postdoc/PhD/IT admin), organization, directory or candidacy link, project name + start date, a 250-word proposal (Google Doc link or paste) covering problem, GCP tools, timeline, milestones, and future GCP use, a field of research, a pricing-calculator estimate with its URL, a GCP billing account ID, and your post-credit funding plan. 3) On approval, redeem within 60 days of the project start date.
  - *Expiry:* Redeem within 60 days of project start; valid 365 days from redemption or until $5,000 used, whichever first; renewal not guaranteed.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are for the described project only — no personal or commercial use; you must declare how you'll fund the project after the credit expires; proposals without a GCP cost estimate get bounced; GPAR is a separate expert-collaboration program, not a second credit pool.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/researchersandhttps://edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/
- **Free student learning offers: 200 Google Skills credits + free hands-on labs, skill badges, Google Cloud Certificates (preview), Google Skills Arcade, Developer Student Groups** (medium) — cloud.google.com/edu/students (read today): students can request 200 free Google Skills credits which expire one year after issue; no-cost access to hands-on labs; skill badges (complete a lab series + challenge-lab final assessment for a shareable credential); Google Cloud Certificates (preview free on YouTube, no prerequisites); Google Skills Arcade by Google Cloud (gamified learning, swag rewards); free Developer Student Groups (join or create a campus chapter); free Cloud OnAir events and YouTube tutorials. There is no .edu-gated GCP compute-credit pool for individual students — students' actual GCP compute comes from faculty teaching credits, the generic $300 trial (CC required), or institutional agreements; the notebook/lab front on GCP therefore runs through faculty credits or trial, while self-serve free lab environments come from Google Skills' sized sandboxes. Confidence is medium on exactly what 200 Skills credits purchase: the page does not publish a per-course price list.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to students (no .edu email explicitly required to request the Google Skills learning credits or badges; DSGs are campus-based). The $300 trial and free tier remain available to students but the trial needs a card. Google's own faculty docs steer students toward faculty-issued credits instead of the trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to cloud.google.com/edu/students → 'Request credits' for the 200 Google Skills credits (learning-platform credits; expire 1 year after issue). 2) Use Google Skills to open the free hands-on labs and earn skill badges. 3) Join/apply to a Developer Student Group or attend Cloud OnAir events (both free). 4) For real GCP compute in coursework, use your faculty's teaching credits or a campus institutional agreement.
  - *Expiry:* Skills credits expire 1 year after issue; labs/badges are free as long as offered.
  - *Gotchas:* '200 Google Skills credits' are learning-platform credits, not GCP $-credits — don't conflate with the $300 trial; Skills GCP sandboxes are time-boxed/limited; there is no free GCP GPU/TPU access for students (GPUs/TPUs are explicitly outside the free tier).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Gemini API / Google AI Studio free tier (the ML-inference + notebooks front; no credit card)** (medium) — ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits (read today; page 'Last updated 2026-10-15 UTC'): usage tiers are Free, then paid Tiers 1–3 (with $10/$50/$200 rolling 10-minute spend caps and escalating limits). Free tier qualification: 'an active project or free trial' — no active billing account required, in contrast to Tier 1+ which require a linked billing account. Per-model free RPD/RPM/TPM numbers are not published on that page; they are shown per-project in the Google AI Studio rate-limit dashboard. Batch API free-tier table starts at Tier 1 (no free batch numbers shown). No free fine-tuning/training credits are listed on the page. Related: the GCP $300 trial credit cannot pay Gemini API-in-AI-Studio costs (trial docs), and the startup program's 'prototyping/Start' offering gives pre-funded builders instant AI Studio access (API keys + Gemini, Gemini Omni, Nano Banana, etc.) plus 'credits for frontier models like Gemini and Gemma' in funded tiers, while third-party partner models are billed directly. Google Colab remains the classic free notebooks front (CPU and quota-limited TPU runtimes), but its current exact quotas could not be re-verified in this session (web-search budget was exhausted) — treat remembered Colab numbers as low confidence.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Google account; the Gemini API free tier needs no credit card and no billing account (Tier 1+ does). GCP trial users: AI Studio Gemini costs are excluded from the $300 trial credit.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign in at aistudio.google.com (or colab.research.google.com for notebooks). 2) Create/select a project, generate an API key, pick a model — free-tier quotas apply automatically. 3) Check per-model free limits in the AI Studio rate-limit dashboard; exceeding them moves you to paid tiers (which require a billing account).
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry for the free tier (ongoing, subject to Google's policy changes).
  - *Gotchas:* Free-tier rates are per-project, per-model and can change; the dashboard is the source of truth; no free batch API at the Free tier; no free fine-tuning stated; Colab TPU sessions are quota/time-limited — don't assume old Colab quotas are current; past free limits, paid tier 1 applies its $10 rolling spend-cap structure rather than unlimited use.
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits
- **Google Cloud Free Trial: $300 credit for 90 days (new customers)** (high) — Signup creates a Free Trial billing account preloaded with a $300 Welcome credit valid for 90 days (cloud.google.com/free; signup FAQ). The trial ends at the first of: $300 spent, 90 days elapsed, or upgrade to a paid account (Free Trial Terms section 1.3, last modified 2026-02-12). Free Tier usage is not charged against the credit, and the credit can cover usage above Free Tier limits. On the trial, Compute Engine is capped at 8 concurrent cores (section 3.1) and some services are unavailable (section 3.3). Upgrading keeps unused credit, which still expires 3 months after the trial start (section 4.2). When the trial ends, access stops; there are 30 days to migrate data (except Compute Engine) or upgrade (section 5.2).
  - *Eligibility:* New Google Cloud customers only: never a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform or Firebase, and never previously signed up for the Free Trial (official docs). US and/or Mexican federal, state or local government entities are excluded unless Google agrees in writing (section 1.2). No .edu or student-email rule; a student qualifies as an individual. No startup age or funding rule applies. A payment method is required (see gotchas). Some countries also require bank account verification.
  - *How to claim:* Open cloud.google.com/free and click Get started for free (or go to console.cloud.google.com/freetrial), sign in with a Google account, complete the Free Trial signup, add a valid payment method and verify identity, then create a project. The 90-day clock starts when the billing account is created. The Firebase console also routes eligible users into this signup (since March 2025).
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from billing account creation, or earlier at $300 spend or upgrade. Unused credit expires at day 90 (3 months after start if upgraded).
  - *Gotchas:* A credit card or other valid payment method is REQUIRED. It is not charged, but authorization holds can stay 1 to 14 business days. This is the key difference from the education credit programs, which need no card. Google may modify or terminate the trial at any time (section 3.6). An expired or invalid card can suspend the account (section 3.5). Faculty pages warn that this trial needs a card, so students should be steered to the education credits instead.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google Cloud Free Tier: always-free monthly limits (20+ products)** (high) — More than 20 products have monthly free usage. The allowance does not expire but is subject to change, and it is not charged against the $300 trial credit (cloud.google.com/free). Examples from the current list: Compute Engine 1 e2-micro instance per month; Cloud Storage 5 GB-months Standard; BigQuery 1 TB of queries per month; Cloud Run 2 million requests per month; one GKE Autopilot or Zonal cluster per month; Cloud Run functions 2 million invocations per month; Pub/Sub 10 GB of messages per month; Firestore 1 GB storage; Cloud Build 120 build-minutes per day; Speech-to-Text 60 minutes per month; Vision AI 1,000 units per month; Natural Language API 5,000 units per month; Secret Manager 6 secret versions per month; Cloud Shell 5 GB disk; Artifact Registry 0.5 GB per month; Workflows 5,000 steps per month; Agent Engine 180,000 vCPU-seconds and 360,000 GiB-seconds per month. Compute Engine detail (official docs): the e2-micro is free only in us-west1 (Oregon), us-central1 (Iowa) and us-east1 (South Carolina); it includes 30 GB-months of standard persistent disk and 1 GB per month of outbound North America data; the limit is by time, not per instance; GPUs and TPUs are excluded. Cloud Storage free usage applies to US regions (the docs name us-east1, us-west1, us-central1).
  - *Eligibility:* Any Google Cloud customer; the trial is not required, and the docs say existing customers can use it too. No .edu or student requirement; no startup rules. Region limits: the e2-micro and Cloud Storage allowances apply only in the listed US regions.
  - *How to claim:* Create a project and Cloud Billing account in the console, create resources only in the free regions, and stay within each monthly limit. Set a Cloud Billing budget with alerts so overages are noticed.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry. Monthly limits reset each month. Google says the limits can change.
  - *Gotchas:* Usage above a free limit is billed at standard prices. Creating the e2-micro outside the three US regions is billed. GPUs and TPUs are never free here. Egress beyond 1 GB per month is billed. Whether a card is needed for free-tier-only use was not verified in this run.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Product-level free trials (GKE, Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB, Looker, Bigtable, Gemini Enterprise)** (medium) — Trial offers listed on cloud.google.com/free: GKE, full capabilities free for 90 days with no fee; Spanner, 90-day trial instance with 10 GB storage; Cloud SQL, 30-day trial instance with 8 vCPU Enterprise Plus, 64 GB memory and 100 GB storage; AlloyDB, 30-day trial with an 8 vCPU basic primary instance and up to 1 TB storage; Looker, 30-day trial instance with standard features; Bigtable, 10-day trial with a 1-node SSD cluster and up to 500 GB; Gemini Enterprise Plus edition, 30 days at no cost. The Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB and Bigtable entries also say new customers get $300 credit on signup.
  - *Eligibility:* Set per product on each trial page; not verified one by one. No .edu requirement. Startup age and funding rules do not apply.
  - *How to claim:* From cloud.google.com/free, open the product's trial link ('Explore more offers') and create the trial instance in the console.
  - *Expiry:* Trial instance periods are 10 days (Bigtable), 30 days (Cloud SQL, AlloyDB, Looker, Gemini Enterprise Plus) or 90 days (GKE, Spanner).
  - *Gotchas:* Trial instances end when their period ends; check each product page for what happens next. Card requirements and region limits for each trial were not checked individually. Trial instances are separate from Free Tier usage.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Students: 200 Google Skills training credits** (high) — Currently enrolled college and university students can request 200 Google Skills credits for hands-on labs, skill badges and courses on Google Skills, the platform formerly called Google Cloud Skills Boost (cloud.google.com/edu/students). Credits expire one year after they are issued. The same page lists Google Skills Arcade (no-cost gamified labs) and a no-cost YouTube preview of Google Cloud Certificates.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified college or university students at regionally accredited institutions in the listed countries. The list is long and includes the US, UK, Canada, Germany, India, Japan, Brazil, Mexico, Australia and Singapore. For-profit institutions are not supported (support.google.com/google-cloud-higher-ed/answer/10322652). The help text does not spell out a .edu rule or the verification method. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Submit the Google Skills training-credits application form (services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudskillsbooststudenttrainingcreditsapplication/) with your institutional details. Requests can take up to two weeks. For more credits, use the same form. Then use the credits on skills.google.
  - *Expiry:* One year after issue.
  - *Gotchas:* These are lab and training credits for Google Skills. The official pages do not describe them as Cloud Billing dollars, so they do not fund your own Cloud projects. The portal domain changed to skills.google in October 2025 (see sunset_notes).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Skills no-cost Starter subscription: 35 credits a month** (medium) — The Starter subscription costs nothing and gives learners 35 credits each month for hands-on labs in Google Skills (support.google.com/qwiklabs/answer/9139481). Google's Skills announcements and the Developer Program FAQ also cite 35 monthly credits for GEAR program participants and Google Cloud Innovators community members. Paid Monthly Pro and Annual Developer Premium plans sit above it.
  - *Eligibility:* Any learner with a Google account. Not limited to students, and no .edu rule. Innovators membership is joined through My Communities and Programs on the developer profile. The card requirement for the no-cost plan was not verified.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in at skills.google, open the profile menu, go to Settings, then Subscriptions, and select the Starter (no cost) plan. For the Innovators or GEAR route, join through the program pages.
  - *Expiry:* Monthly allotment. Whether unused credits roll over was not verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are for labs only. The exact reset and rollover rules, and whether the free plan asks for a card, were not checked directly.
  - *Source:* https://support.google.com/qwiklabs/answer/9139481?hl=en
- **Google Career Launchpad: no-cost AI and cloud training for higher education** (medium) — Career Launchpad gives higher-education institutions (and government and nonprofit workforce organizations) no-cost generative-AI and cloud training. It includes DeepMind's AI Research Foundations curriculum, Google Cloud Certificates, hands-on labs and Educator Guides. Faculty also get Google Skills credits to add labs to coursework. Google Skills Arcade is always-on and no-cost. Google Cloud Certificates carry an ACE recommendation of up to 10 college credits (page footnote). Career Dreamer is US-only. The page mentions a 50% certification discount for some learning-path completers; conditions were not checked.
  - *Eligibility:* Institutional program run by faculty facilitators at higher-education institutions. The page sets no .edu rule. Career Dreamer is US-only. The country list was not checked. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Faculty apply through the Apply button on cloud.google.com/edu/faculty/career-launchpad (faculty form: services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudcareerlaunchpadapplication/).
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page. Expiry for the Skills credits within this program was not confirmed.
  - *Gotchas:* This is non-cash training, not a credit balance. The certification discount and ACE college-credit conditions were not verified.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty/career-launchpad
- **Education (teaching) credits: up to $100 per teaching staff member and $50 per student** (high) — Eligible faculty can apply for up to $100 in Google Cloud credits per teaching staff member and up to $50 in Cloud credits per student (cloud.google.com/edu/faculty). Approval issues coupons: faculty get codes for themselves and staff, plus a URL to send to students. Each course's credit is tracked in its own Cloud Billing account. Credits cover most Google Cloud products, and free-tier usage does not count against them (Cloud Billing edu-grants doc). Credits may not be used for course infrastructure, commercial use or personal use.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only; students cannot apply but can ask faculty to apply. Faculty must use an official school email on a valid .edu domain (or nominate another email if they have none). In the US the institution must be a regionally accredited program; outside the US it must be a higher-learning institution that awards degrees in an eligible country. For-profit institutions are excluded. The application form has a country list (includes the US, UK, Canada, India, Japan, Germany, Australia and Brazil). Up to 3 courses per submission, with course start no later than one year away. Students must confirm a school email to redeem. The support page says sign-up needs no credit card.
  - *How to claim:* Faculty apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching (up to 3 courses, expected student and TA counts, school faculty-directory link). After approval, faculty get coupon codes and a student URL. Students verify their school email, then click Redeem now in the coupon email and accept the terms in the Google Cloud console. Questions go to CloudEduGrants@google.com.
  - *Expiry:* Redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after course start. Validity sources conflict: up to 1 year after course start (program page), 12 months after course start (help page), and 1 year after redemption (Cloud Billing docs). Confirm against the approval email.
  - *Gotchas:* The official amounts are per teaching staff ($100) and per student ($50). The '$100 per course' wording in the brief is not how the official page states it. Credits are non-transferable and tied to the school email. Google may revoke awards or change criteria at any time. Check spam for the confirmation email, which should arrive within 24 hours.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Research credits: up to $5,000 for faculty, PhD students and postdocs** (medium) — Researchers can apply for up to $5,000 in Google Cloud research credits (cloud.google.com/edu/researchers says 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'; the application page text retrieved did not restate the figure). The credit must be activated within 60 days of the project start date on the application. It may be issued in increments and is for proof of concept, evaluation or comparison, workshops or training, and open-source development. The application asks for a funding plan for after the credit expires.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty, PhD students and postdoctoral researchers at accredited higher-education institutions, plus eligible nonprofit research institutions, in approved countries (the list includes the US, UK, Canada, Germany, Japan, India, Australia, Singapore and South Africa). An institution account is preferred but not strictly .edu. PhD students may not use the credit for personal use. The GPAR program (Google Public Sector Program for Accelerated Research) is US-only and gives pricing co-investment, not credit. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research. Provide personal and organization details, a project proposal (up to 250 words), the total from your pricing calculator with a link to it, a Google Cloud billing account ID, and a funding plan for after the credit expires.
  - *Expiry:* Expires 365 days after coupon redemption or when fully used, whichever comes first.
  - *Gotchas:* Non-transferable, with no sale or barter and no commercial use. The $5,000 headline comes from the researchers landing page. Confirm the cap on the form before relying on it, since 'up to' means awards can be smaller.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/researchers
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program, Start tier: up to $2,000 in credits** (high) — The Start tier gives up to $2,000 in Google Cloud credits valid for one year (cloud.google.com/startup/benefits). Also: $200 in Google Skills training credits; 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free for new sign-ups (conditions apply); AI webinars and content; and Web3 partner perks for Web3 startups. This is the self-serve entry tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Technology startup with a working MVP, a clear business model and a well-defined product roadmap, that plans to seek venture funding soon. Founded within the last 24 months. Has not received Google Cloud credits beyond the free trial. The business email must match the company website domain, and the billing account must be owned and administered by someone on that domain. Not open to educational institutions, nonprofits, government entities, dev shops or consultancies, companies that have IPO'd or been acquired, or crypto mining. Google decides acceptance at its sole discretion. A Cloud Billing account is required; card requirement not stated.
  - *How to claim:* Create a Cloud Billing account in the console and note its 18-character billing account ID, since credits attach to it. Confirm a business email on your website domain. Apply through the Apply now button at cloud.google.com/startup. On approval, credits are issued to the billing account named in the application.
  - *Expiry:* Valid one year (official page). Some third-party sites say two years; the official page does not.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits apply only to Google first-party pay-as-you-go or consumption products. Subscription, prepay and annual-license products are excluded, as are third-party Marketplace and Model Garden offerings. Third-party models (such as Claude) are billed directly. Credits cannot be shared, split or resold. Workspace is free only if the company domain had no paid Workspace plan in the 31 days before applying. Re-applying is possible if earlier credits went unused.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program, Scale tier: up to $200,000 (AI-first up to $350,000)** (high) — Scale tier: up to $200,000 in Google Cloud credits over two years. Year 1 covers 100% of spend up to $100,000; year 2 covers 20% of spend up to a further $100,000. AI-first startups: up to $350,000 (year 1 100% up to $250,000; year 2 20% up to $100,000 more). Also: 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free (separate application), $600 per month of Google Maps Platform credits (separate application), up to $12,000 in Google Cloud Enhanced Support credits for one year, Google Skills training credits (the benefits page lists $500 for this tier; verify), and a Startup Success Manager as you grow.
  - *Eligibility:* Venture-backed startups. Pre-Seed or Seed rounds within the last 5 years; if a Series A has been raised, it must be within the last 12 months. Founded within the last five years (benefits page; the program footnote says 3 to 5 years depending on tier). Has not received more than $5,000 in Google Cloud credits before. Non-venture funding (private equity, bank loans, government grants, crowdfunding, friends and family) does not qualify. Stealth startups are accepted on investor or accelerator verification. The AI-first tier requires Gemini or Gemma as a foundation of the primary product (AI program page). Must be in a region where Google Cloud is generally available and sold through self-service purchasing. Acceptance is at Google's discretion, weighing funding stage and type, business model, founder experience, product usage, age, growth and total funding. No .edu rule. A billing account is required; card requirement not stated.
  - *How to claim:* Have your funding round details, any investor or accelerator affiliation (enter these for stealth verification), billing account ID and website-domain business email ready. Apply at cloud.google.com/startup (Apply now). Credits are issued to the billing account after approval. Maps and Workspace benefits need separate applications.
  - *Expiry:* Credits run over two years in year-1 and year-2 tranches. The exact expiry of unused balances was not in the official text read.
  - *Gotchas:* Same first-party-only exclusions and third-party model billing as the Start tier. Credits cannot be shared, split or resold. Prior credits above $5,000 disqualify. Some third-party guides say the Scale tier is usually reached through a partner investor or accelerator; the official page does not say so. Approval is not guaranteed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Startups in prototyping: free Gemini API access in Google AI Studio** (medium) — The prototyping track of the Google for Startups program gives free access to the Gemini API in Google AI Studio, with API key management, access to Google models such as Gemini, Gemini Omni and Nano Banana, and Build mode (cloud.google.com/startup and startup.google.com/cloud). This is product access, not a Cloud Billing credit.
  - *Eligibility:* Developers and builders at small businesses, bootstrapped startups and self-funded ideas, which are the stated criteria. No venture funding is required. No .edu rule. Card and regional requirements were not verified.

The Start tier's $2,000 is valid for one year.

The 12-month term applies to the $2,000 Start tier. AI startups can reach $350,000.

### Google Colab Free Tier

- **Colab Free Tier (Notebooks) — free CPU/GPU/TPU runtimes** (high) — Browser-hosted Jupyter notebooks, free of charge, with CPU-only and free GPU + TPU runtime options. Free sessions run for at most 12 hours; idle VMs are deleted after inactivity. No fixed/published compute quota — Google states the specific GPU and TPU types available "vary over time" (community-reported free GPU is commonly an NVIDIA T4, 16 GB; official FAQ does not name a guaranteed model). No credit card required — sign in with a Google account. Free (no compute-unit balance) runtimes are shared/preemptible and prohibit: file hosting, torrent/P2P, remote shells & desktops, bypassing the notebook UI, remote proxies, crypto mining, DoS, password cracking, multi-account abuse, deepfakes, distributed-compute workers, and containerization to evade anti-abuse checks.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a Google account. No .edu email required, no region restriction stated in the FAQ, and no credit card required.
  - *How to claim:* Go to colab.research.google.com, sign in with any Google account, open/create a notebook, then Runtime > Change runtime type > Hardware accelerator = GPU or TPU. No signup form or payment.
  - *Expiry:* No subscription expiry. Each session is capped at ~12 hours; idle VMs are deleted. Notebooks/models persist only if saved to Google Drive (Drive free-tier 15 GB shared).
  - *Gotchas:* Free GPU/TPU are shared and intermittently unavailable (no guaranteed quota; may be interrupted under high demand). T4 vs TPU specifics not officially fixed. Paid products (Colab Pro / Pro+ / Pay-As-You-Go, billed on a compute-unit balance; Pro+ up to 24 h continuous with enough units) are separate from free — do not conflate. Free runtimes carry the anti-abuse restrictions above.
  - *Source:* https://research.google.com/colaboratory/faq.html
- **Colab Pro for Education (students & faculty)** (high) — Formerly a FREE 1-year Colab Pro subscription for students and faculty of US-based universities (more compute than the free tier). Per the official FAQ it is now CLOSED: "no longer available for new signups at this time." Previously-verified education users keep access until their 1-year anniversary.
  - *Eligibility:* Was: US-based university students/faculty (typically .edu verification). Now: new signups not accepted.
  - *How to claim:* No longer claimable for new users. Existing verified education users retain access until their 1-year subscription end.
  - *Expiry:* Existing subscriptions expire at the 1-year mark; new signups are closed. Upgrading to a paid personal plan permanently unsubscribes the free education benefit.
  - *Gotchas:* Sunset offer. Do not pay for a personal Colab Pro/Pro+ assuming it replaces the education benefit — doing so terminates the free edu subscription permanently.
  - *Source:* https://research.google.com/colaboratory/faq.html
- **No Colab-specific startup program or Colab trial credits (GCP credits do NOT apply)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — There is no Colab-specific startup program and no free Colab trial-credit offer. The adjacent offers are Google Cloud ones: a Startup Program (tiered credits, roughly $500 up to ~$100K) and a new-account Free Trial (historically ~$300 over ~90 days, credit card typically required). These credits apply to GCP services (GCE, Vertex AI, GKE, BigQuery, etc.), NOT to Colab's free tier or to Colab Pro/Pro+ subscriptions.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup Program: for-profit company, founded within the last ~3 years, raised under ~$50M, in a supported region. Free Trial: new GCP account (CC historically required). Neither grants free Colab.
  - *How to claim:* Not claimable for Colab. To use the credits you run self-hosted GCP workloads (Vertex AI / GKE / GCE) instead of Colab.
  - *Expiry:* Startup credits valid ~3 months from issuance; free trial ~90 days.
  - *Gotchas:* Could not re-verify live GCP figures today (cloud.google.com/programs/startup returned 404; /free and /colab/pricing were truncated/JS-rendered) — treat the credit amounts and caps as approximate/low-confidence. Colab free tier itself needs no CC and no credits.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/programs/startup
- **2025–26 change:** Sunset / materially changed (2025–2026): (1) COLAB PRO FOR EDUCATION — the formerly free 1-year Colab Pro subscription for US university students/faculty is now CLOSED to new signups (official FAQ, read 2026-10-22); only pre-verified users keep access until their 1-year anniversary, and upgrading to a paid personal plan terminates it permanently. (2) NO COLAB-STARTUP OFFER — there is no Colab-specific startup program or free Colab trial-credit; the only \"credits\" are Google Cloud Startup Program / new-account Free Trial, which do NOT apply to Colab. Method note: web-search budget was exhausted this session, so 2025–2026 change-detection relied on the official FAQ fetched today plus best-effort GCP fetches; GCP credit amounts/eligibility could not be re-verified live (target pages 404'd or truncated), so treat those figures as approximate.

### GPUStack

- **Free open-source self-hosted runtime (the only real free offer; available to students and startups alike)** (high) — GPUStack core is free, open-source, Apache 2.0 licensed (NOT MIT as the task's 'known so far' said — both gpustack.ai and the GitHub repo state Apache 2.0 today). One-line Docker install (server: sudo docker run -d --name gpustack -p 80:80 --volume gpustack-data:/var/lib/gpustack gpustack/gpustack; worker: separate privileged Docker command with --server-url and a generated --token). Includes Ollama-style local web UI + OpenAI/Anthropic-compatible API, model deployment, and supports heterogeneous accelerators (NVIDIA; AMD/ROCm, Ascend, Hygon, MetaX, Moore Threads, Cambricon, Iluvatar, T-Head PPU per the site). Free forever, no expiry. No hosted free tier, no trial credits, no student/education offer, no startup program exists on the official site (verified /, /enterprise, /blog, GitHub, docs quickstart on 2026-10-22; /cloud/ and /pricing/ are 404). Sources read today: https://gpustack.ai, https://gpustack.ai/enterprise, https://gpustack.ai/blog/, https://gpustack.ai/blog/introducing-gpustack/, https://github.com/gpustack/gpustack, https://docs.gpustack.ai/latest/quickstart/
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone — students, startups, enterprises, individuals. No .edu email, no account, no company age or funding-cap requirements, no region limits. Credit card NOT required; no commercial license, no login/account to set up (local 'admin' user with a system-generated password).
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim or sign up for: clone/download the open-source code (https://github.com/gpustack/gpustack) and run the Docker one-liners from https://docs.gpustack.ai/latest/quickstart/. Worker nodes need Linux + at least one GPU (docs quickstart covers NVIDIA; site advertises AMD/ROCm and Chinese accelerators too; macOS/Windows can host the server via Docker Desktop, workers are Linux-only; Windows via WSL2).
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite — Apache 2.0 license is perpetual; no sunsetting date for the open-source product.
  - *Gotchas:* The 'free compute' is BYO-hardware: you pay for your own GPU, electricity, and (for model pulls from hub sources) data egress; there is no GPUStack-hosted free GPU pool. Workers are Linux-only — no macOS worker nodes. RBAC, SSO (OIDC/SAML/LDAP), token quotas, metering & billing, white-label, HA and multi-tenancy are Enterprise features sold via 'Talk to Sales'/'Book a Demo' (https://gpustack.ai/book-demo) with no published pricing — do not assume free. The June 2026 'Introducing GPUStack' blog repositions the product as an 'enterprise AI infrastructure platform' with 'MaaS and GPUaaS under one control plane' (https://gpustack.ai/blog/introducing-gpustack/), so a commercial hosted GPUaaS is signaled, but no public self-serve page, pricing, or free tier for it was found (gpustack.ai/cloud and gpustack.ai/pricing return 404). WebSearch was unavailable this session (session budget exhausted), so third-party sources (funding news, off-site discount resellers) could not be checked.
  - *Source:* https://gpustack.ai
- **GPUStack Enterprise (paid, sales-led — NOT a free offer; listed so it is not confused with a free tier)** (medium) — Enterprise adds high availability, multi-tenancy, RBAC/SSO, token quotas, rate limits, audit logs, metering & billing, white-label. No dollar figures, no tiers, no trial credits, no self-serve purchase anywhere on gpustack.ai; the only CTA is 'Talk to Sales' → /book-demo. A free trial or evaluation sandbox may be negotiated via sales but is not publicly published. Sources: https://gpustack.ai/enterprise, https://gpustack.ai/book-demo
  - *Eligibility:* Enterprise/sales channel; no public self-serve. No student or startup eligibility criteria published.
  - *How to claim:* Book a demo at https://gpustack.ai/book-demo/ to request evaluation or pricing; nothing claimable online.
  - *Expiry:* n/a (no published free component)
  - *Gotchas:* Verify what is actually free vs. Enterprise-locked before self-hosting in production; the metering/billing and white-label features are on the paid side of the line.
  - *Source:* https://gpustack.ai/enterprise
- **2025–26 change:** No student, education, or startup program existed to sunset, and no trial-credit program was closed, in 2025-2026 — GPUStack has run exclusively as (a) a free Apache-2.0 self-hosted runtime and (b) a sales-led Enterprise product, with no published pricing pages (/pricing/ and /cloud/ are 404 as of 2026-10-22). Material 2025-2026 changes to note: (1) the task's 'known so far' claim of an MIT license is wrong — both the official site and the GitHub repo say Apache 2.0 today; (2) November 2025 'v2' and March 2026 'v2.1' releases added faster inference, Alibaba T-Head PPU support, a model gateway with routing, and a community backend marketplace (per https://gpustack.ai/blog/ listing; individual release details not fetched, so medium confidence on the specifics); (3) in June 2026 GPUStack repositioned as an 'enterprise AI infrastructure platform' marketing 'MaaS and GPUaaS under one control plane' (https://gpustack.ai/blog/introducing-gpustack/) — a hosted GPUaaS is signaled but had no public self-serve page, pricing, or free tier as of 2026-10-22. The original https://gpustack.co domain does not resolve; gpustack.ai is the live official domain. Caveat: the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted, so all 'no program exists' conclusions rest on official-page fetches done today only; an off-site program (e.g., a partner marketplace discount) could not be ruled out via search.

### Hugging Face

- **Inference Providers free account: NO monthly credits (as of Oct 2026)** (high) — Official pricing docs (read 2026-10-22): Free Users row in the included-credits table is 'None' — free accounts get no monthly compute credits. Free users can still access Inference Providers (18 providers, 200+ models: Baseten, Cerebras, Cohere, DeepInfra, Fal, Featherless, Fireworks, Groq, HF Inference, Novita, Nscale, OVHcloud, Public AI, Replicate, Scaleway, Together, WaveSpeed, Z.ai) via pay-as-you-go 'credits purchase required', or via a custom provider key. The PRO marketing page claims free-tier users get a 100k token/month inference usage cap vs 2M for PRO, but the pricing docs define no free credit and I could not find a published limits table — treat the 100k figure as marketing, not docs.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Hugging Face account (free, global, no .edu, no credit card to create the account or to browse; a credit card IS required before any pay-as-you-go usage, since extra usage requires purchasing credits via Stripe). No student or startup special tier.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at huggingface.co, generate a fine-grained token with 'Make calls to Inference Providers' permission; add a credit card in Billing settings and purchase credits to exceed the free usage cap.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing; no stated expiry
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: there is no trial-credit offer on Inference Providers — the only ever-free allowance was $0.10/month for free accounts, discontinued 2026-10-07 (docs update). No credit card stated as required at sign-up, but one is required to buy pay-as-you-go credits. Monthly tokens cap (100k, unverified in docs) is a usage limit, not a free grant.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/inference-providers/en/pricing
- **Custom Provider Key: $0 on Hugging Face (bring-your-own provider key)** (high) — Documented in the official pricing page (read 2026-10-22): you can set a custom provider key in HF settings (or pass it to the InferenceClient / OpenAI-compatible client) and HF routes requests for free — 'Hugging Face will not charge you for the call'; billing goes directly to the provider you already pay. This is the only genuinely card-free-on-HF way to use Inference Providers.
  - *Eligibility:* All account types incl. free. You need your own account/key with the provider; no region limit stated; no credit card needed on HF.
  - *How to claim:* Settings → Inference Providers → add custom provider key, or pass the key to InferenceClient; code and OpenAI-compatible SDK usage are unchanged.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing
  - *Gotchas:* Included monthly credits do NOT apply to custom-provider-key usage. The provider's own free tier/trial terms now govern cost — this is a 'no markup' pass-through, not a free allowance. If the provider later requires its own payment method, the 'free' path ends there.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/inference-providers/en/pricing
- **PRO plan: $9/mo subscription including $2.00/mo compute credits (paid, not free)** (high) — PRO ($9/month, credit card only, Stripe, no stated trial) includes $2.00/month of general-purpose compute credits usable on Inference Providers, Inference Endpoints, upgraded CPU/GPU Spaces hardware incl. ZeroGPU usage beyond the 5-min free daily quota, and Jobs. PRO also raises the inference usage cap to 2M tokens/mo (vs 100k free claim), 1 TB private storage (base price $12-18/TB/mo above), 10 ZeroGPU Spaces, 8× daily ZeroGPU quota (40 min RTX Pro 6000 Blackwell), dev-mode Spaces. No student discount, no free PRO tier, no student-specific enrollment found on huggingface.co/pro or /pricing today.
  - *Eligibility:* Any user globally; credit card required for the subscription. No .edu requirement, no regional restriction stated.
  - *How to claim:* Subscribe at https://huggingface.co/subscribe/pro; credits are credited monthly and applied automatically before pay-as-you-go billing.
  - *Expiry:* Renews monthly; cancel anytime in billing settings. Docs say credits are 'credited every month' but do not promise rollover of unused credits.
  - *Gotchas:* The $2 credit does not cover the $9 subscription. 'Pay Pro with credits' was reportedly possible in the past and now appears removed (HF forums, Feb 2026). Not a student/startup offer.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/pro
- **Team plan: $20/user/mo incl. $2/seat/mo shared credits (paid, not free)** (high) — Team orgs ($20/user/mo; self-serve, credit card or AWS/Google Cloud Marketplace billing) get $2.00/month compute credits per seat, shared across members, spendable on Inference Providers, Inference Endpoints, Spaces upgrades and Jobs. Org admins can set spending limits and disable providers; usage billable centrally via X-HF-Bill-To header or bill_to client parameter.
  - *Eligibility:* Orgs globally; card or cloud-marketplace billing. No startup/education discount published.
  - *How to claim:* Subscribe Team in org settings; members specify bill_to org (or X-HF-Bill-To header) to draw on the shared credit pool.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits only apply to HF-routed usage, not custom provider keys. Not free; included only for completeness of the 'credits' picture.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/inference-providers/en/pricing
- **Six months free PRO for startups — third-party claims only, unverified official** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Several affiliate/promo-code directories (SaaSTweaks 'verified May 2026', xraise.ai 'verified 2026-05-15', startup-perks.com 'audited Feb 2026') claim a 'Hugging Face for Startups' offer of ~6 months of free PRO plus Inference Endpoints credits, applied at huggingface.co/startups. But huggingface.co/startups is actually an org profile page ('startupsdao') with zero program content, and two independent checks of HF-owned pages found no program: guptadeepak (checked 2026-09-13: 'No startup program appears on any Hugging Face owned page') and CloudRoute's 2026-10-09 catalog, which excluded Hugging Face from its 62 verified startup programs because 'no official source confirmed a current program'.
  - *Eligibility:* Unverifiable — no published criteria on any HF page. Aggregators loosely describe 'early-stage AI startups', pre-seed to Series A, global; some mention 'oss' targeting.
  - *How to claim:* No official application path exists today. The claimed route (promo code at huggingface.co/startups) leads to an org page, not a program. Contact sales via /contact/sales if pursuing, or treat the offer as unconfirmed.
  - *Gotchas:* Affiliate-driven content; 'vendor-published criteria' cited by these sites are not on any huggingface.co page I could read today. Do not plan around this offer without written confirmation from HF.
  - *Source:* https://saastweaks.com/startup-credits/hugging-face-pro-credits
- **Academia Hub organization tier — exists in docs, no public free offer** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — The Hub rate-limits docs (tiers table dated Sept '25) list an 'Academia Hub organization' tier with the same 5-minute rate limits as a Team org (3,000 API / 20,000 resolvers / 400 pages per 5 min), implying HF runs a dedicated academic org plan. However no huggingface.co page I read today (pricing, enterprise, pro, education) publishes an Academia plan, a student discount, an education license, a .edu-email program, or a free academic tier. huggingface.co/education is just an org profile ('High School' org, 15 followers, no program content). A community dataset notes the 'huggingface.co/community-grants' URL was 404 as of 2026-Q2 and no public grant application path existed; the /pricing page mentions 'community GPU grants for side projects' without a link.
  - *Eligibility:* Unknown/presumably universities & research institutions via contract or sales; no public eligibility, no .edu requirement published.
  - *How to claim:* No public claim path found; would be via /contact/sales. Treat as unavailable for self-serve students.
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* This is an internal tier name surfaced in docs, not an offer. No amounts, no free access, no application form — do not cite it as an education license.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/en/rate-limits
- **Free static Spaces hosting (unlimited)** (high) — Static Spaces (plain HTML, or built React/Svelte/Vue) are $0, free for everyone, unlimited in number, with no hardware charge and no card required. Default per-Space limits: 2 vCPU / 16 GB RAM / 50 GB non-persistent disk; CPU Basic (2 vCPU / 16 GB) has 'no hourly cost' but as of mid-2026 creating a compute Space (Gradio or Docker, incl. CPU Basic) requires a paid plan (PRO $9/mo personal; Team $20/Enterprise $50 per user for orgs). Static SDK Spaces never require a paid plan. All free for anonymous visitors too; no egress fee noted for Spaces.
  - *Eligibility:* Any account, free; no credit card, no age/region limit stated for static Spaces. No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Create a Space with the Static SDK at huggingface.co/spaces -> 'Create new Space', push your site; it deploys free forever. Zero setup cost, card only needed if you later add paid hardware.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite while account exists; auto-rebuild on each push. Space goes to sleep when idle.
  - *Gotchas:* As of ~July 2026 free accounts can no longer create Gradio/Docker compute Spaces (incl. free CPU Basic) — static only; CPU Basic downgrade from ZeroGPU also requires PRO. Non-persistent 50GB disk, 16GB RAM ceiling. PRO is $9/mo card-only; free tier has no hourly charge but compute creation is gated.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/spaces-overview
- **ZeroGPU free GPU demos (host up to 2 Spaces + free usage quota)** (high) — ZeroGPU dynamically allocates NVIDIA RTX Pro 6000 Blackwell (half = 48GB VRAM 'large', full = 96GB 'xlarge', xlarge costs 2x quota). Free personal accounts 'in good standing' (verified email, account older than 30 days) can HOST up to 2 ZeroGPU Spaces at $0. Visitors get daily GPU quotas: unauthenticated 2 min, free account 5 min, PRO 40 min (8x), Team 40 min, Enterprise 60 min; quota resets exactly 24h after first usage. Default per-call runtime 60s; over-quota usage $1 per 10 min, but only PRO/Team/Enterprise can extend (pre-paid credits). No card required for the free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Free personal accounts: email verified + account >30 days old; Gradio SDK only; PyTorch-first (transformers/diffusers best). No .edu or startup requirement; no card required.
  - *How to claim:* Create a Gradio Space with ZeroGPU hardware selected, decorate GPU code with @spaces.GPU, push; hosting is free for up to 2 such Spaces. Usage by any visitor is free within their daily 5-min (free account) quota.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite; daily quota resets every 24h. PRO is $9/mo (card required) for 10 Spaces, 40 min quota and overage billing.
  - *Gotchas:* Free accounts cannot buy over-quota credits (paid tiers only). xlarge (96GB) burns 2x quota (45s task = 90s quota). After 2026 hardware migration some workloads auto-fell back to xlarge, doubling effective quota consumption (forum reports May 2026). Queue priority rises as your remaining daily quota falls. Gradio-only; Docker Spaces can't use ZeroGPU. Historically an undocumented run-count limit (~3 runs/day, staff-confirmed April 2026) may still apply on top of the 5-min time quota. A forum answer (July 2026) suggests new free accounts see ZeroGPU as the only creatable runtime — CPU Basic now PRO-gated.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/spaces-zerogpu
- **Community GPU grants (in-kind, rolling)** (medium) — HF offers 'community GPU grants' for side projects: in-kind dedicated GPU hardware that ZeroGPU can't cover (non-PyTorch heavy init, models >96GB VRAM, always-on serving) or an extra Gradio Space beyond the free 2-Space cap without needing PRO. No dollar amount, duration or pool size published; decisions can take days; approved hardware attaches automatically (Static Spaces just flip SDK to gradio).
  - *Eligibility:* Public, open use cases — 'open research demo, hobbyist project, educational tool, institutional showcase' — that 'can't pay for the hardware'. No funding-cap or age limit; free account works; card not needed for the grant itself.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Build your Space (ZeroGPU or Static). 2) Post a Community Tab discussion titled 'Apply for a GPU community grant: <Personal|Company|Academic> project' with a one-paragraph description + justification (default: 'Public open-source demo, can't cover the hardware cost'). 3) Wait for approval; hardware attaches automatically.
  - *Expiry:* Rolling, no published deadline; no published grant duration.
  - *Gotchas:* In-kind only, no cash; approval is discretionary and slow (days+); not a formal startup program; don't rely on a guaranteed quota.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/pricing
- **Hugging Face for Classrooms (free education workspace)** (high) — Free classroom-organization offering: teachers set up a shared workspace where students upload datasets, models and ML demos 'for free', with version control and student access controls (admin/read/write), teaching materials and support. 'Everything is free' per the page; no numeric repo/storage/GPU caps published.
  - *Eligibility:* Any classroom — students and teachers; no .edu verification gate stated, no card required, no institution-type minimum.
  - *How to claim:* Create free at huggingface.co/classrooms ('Create a classroom for free', routed to /organizations/new?orgType=classroom); students join and host demos/datasets in the org workspace at $0.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite while the classroom org exists.
  - *Gotchas:* Compute (GPU) is not part of the free classroom bundle — it's storage/hub hosting of repos, datasets, models and static demos; hardware upgrades are separate paid services. Limits not published, so heavy use could hit general free-tier storage limits.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/classrooms
- **Academia Hub (education license — PAID, free only at partner universities)** (high) — Institution-level education plan: from $10/seat/month with volume discounts; minimum 250 annual seats; includes 1TB private + 1TB public storage per seat, $2/month/seat of compute credits (Inference Providers, Inference Endpoints, upgraded Spaces hardware, Jobs), higher daily ZeroGPU priority, SSO/SCIM/SOC 2. Students get nothing for free as individuals — HF staff confirmed there is no student Pro plan (Oct 2025 forum). Partner universities (e.g., ESCP, 12,000+ students) negotiate free access for their community.
  - *Eligibility:* University IT/procurement must initiate from a university email; individual .edu email is NOT enough. Free only at partner institutions that have negotiated a deal.
  - *How to claim:* Contact HF from your university's IT/procurement team to negotiate; or at a partner university, your admin provisions your seat — then you get PRO-equivalent features + $2/mo compute credits free of personal cost.
  - *Expiry:* Subscription term, renews automatically; per-institution negotiation.
  - *Gotchas:* Not free for most students; the $2/mo compute credits are for paid seats the institution funds; minimum 250 seats excludes most small departments (Team plan suggested instead).
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/academia-hub
- **Startup program: none first-party (third-party 'free PRO for startups' claims unverified)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — No startup program exists on any Hugging Face owned page (checked Sep 2026 by a tracker; pricing/enterprise pages list only PRO/Team/Enterprise with no startup discount or application path). The well-known 2024 '$10M of GPUs' was a one-off commitment at ZeroGPU launch (its free quota persists, the $10M pool did not). Third-party perk marketplaces (creditforstartups, saasoffers, perq.sh) advertise 'up to $108 free PRO via partner perks' or '$1,000 in GPU credits' for HF — none of these could be verified against an HF-owned page; treat as unverified aggregator claims. The old '50% off Enterprise in year one' startup offer is no longer verifiable anywhere on HF's sites.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A for a first-party program; third-party perks claim pre-seed–Series A AI startups worldwide, referral from partner investor/accelerator, but terms aren't published by HF.
  - *How to claim:* There is nothing to claim directly. Startups should use: free static Spaces + 2 free ZeroGPU Spaces per team member, the community GPU grant (in-kind), and generic cloud startup programs (AWS Activate, Google/Microsoft for Startups) for compute instead.
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* Beware unverified marketplace 'credits' (amounts unverifiable, may require equity/referral, may be expired promos). Meta x Hugging Face x Scaleway AI Startup Program (EU accelerator, 2nd cohort Sep 2024–Feb 2025) is now discontinued.
  - *Source:* https://www.makerperks.com/programs/huggingface/hugging-face-for-startups/
- **ZeroGPU free GPU inference (Spaces)** (high) — Shared NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPUs allocated on demand to Gradio Spaces. 'large' = half card, 48GB VRAM (1x quota); 'xlarge' = full card, 96GB VRAM (2x quota). Daily GPU quotas: unauthenticated 2 min; free account 5 min; PRO/Team 40 min; Enterprise 60 min; paid plans can extend via pre-paid credits at $1/10 min. Quota resets exactly 24h after first daily GPU use. No credit card required for the free tier; no stated regional limits. Free accounts host max 2 ZeroGPU Spaces (PRO: 10; orgs: 50). Gradio SDK only; @spaces.GPU decorator, default 60s function runtime (extendable via duration=...); no torch.compile (use PyTorch AOT, torch 2.8+).
  - *Eligibility:* Any free HF account in good standing: verified email address and account older than 30 days. No .edu required, no startup status, no credit card, no stated region limits. Using (visiting) existing ZeroGPU Spaces is free for everyone, even unauthenticated (2 min/day).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at huggingface.co (free), verify email; create a Gradio Space and select ZeroGPU hardware in Space Settings (free accounts: up to 2 such Spaces, allowed only after account is 30+ days old); import spaces and decorate GPU functions with @spaces.GPU (default size large; size='xlarge' for 96GB at 2x quota cost); deploy and use within the 5 min/day free quota.
  - *Expiry:* Quota renews daily (resets 24h after first use). Hosting cap (2 ZeroGPU Spaces) persists while account stays in good standing; no sunset date published for the program.
  - *Gotchas:* Gradio-only (Docker/static cannot use ZeroGPU); xlarge doubles quota consumption and queue wait; free tier cannot buy quota extension ($1/10-min credits only for PRO/Team/Enterprise); model must be loaded to 'cuda' at module level (CUDA is emulated outside @spaces.GPU functions); remaining quota determines queue priority, so idle free users queue behind paid ones; as of 2025-2026 creating new CPU Gradio/Docker Spaces now requires a paid plan - the 2-Space ZeroGPU exception is the only free Gradio path.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/spaces-zerogpu
- **Static Spaces + free Hub tier (free hosting, no GPU)** (high) — Static HTML Spaces are free for everyone on any plan. CPU Basic hardware (2 vCPU/16GB, no accelerator) is $0/hour. Free personal accounts get 100GB private storage (public storage 'best-effort' free), unlimited public models/datasets repos, and free CPU Spaces. No credit card needed. Inference Endpoints/Jobs (paid GPU) are NOT free - there is no free notebook/training offering.
  - *Eligibility:* Any free HF account, worldwide, no verification beyond the account itself; static Spaces need no email-verified/30-day status the way ZeroGPU hosting does.
  - *How to claim:* Create a free account, create a Space with SDK 'static HTML' (or CPU Basic Gradio/Docker if already on a paid plan); no payment setup required.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing free tier; no expiry stated. Note the 2025-2026 change: new Gradio/Docker Spaces (even on free CPU Basic) now require PRO (personal) or Team/Enterprise (orgs) to create or duplicate - only Static Spaces remain free for free accounts.
  - *Gotchas:* The marketing page /classrooms still says demos are 'hosted for free', but the Spaces docs explicitly require a paid plan for new Gradio/Docker Spaces; duplicating a Gradio/Docker Space follows the same paid rule (ZeroGPU exception only). Free spaces sleep when idle; protected/private visibility requires PRO/Team/Enterprise. Free private storage capped at 100GB, overflow is billed.
  - *Source:* https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/spaces-overview
- **Community GPU grants (free dedicated GPU hardware for Spaces)** (medium) — Case-by-case grants that cover Space hardware upgrade costs (dedicated GPU, or ZeroGPU slots beyond the free 2-Space cap) for public-facing community use cases: open research demos, hobbyist projects, educational tools, institutional showcases; company projects may apply ('Company project' option). No fixed dollar amount - the grant attaches the needed hardware to the Space. Official docs link to applying 'using the link in the Settings tab of your Space'. No credit card; no stated startup age/funding caps.
  - *Eligibility:* Qualifying public, non-commercial-leaning Space use cases where the user 'can't pay for the hardware' (open source, research, educational, institutional). Private/commercial/closed-source projects are steered to PRO instead. No .edu required for company grants.
  - *How to claim:* Build the Space (as ZeroGPU if you still have a free slot, else a free Static Space with ZeroGPU-ready code), then open a Community Tab discussion on the Space titled 'Apply for a GPU community grant: <Personal|Company|Academic> project' with one paragraph on what the app does and one on why it should run on free GPU; wait days for review; approved grants attach hardware automatically (Static holding Spaces must flip sdk to gradio). The entry point is also the 'apply for a community GPU grant' link in the Space Settings tab.
  - *Expiry:* Grant covers the attached hardware while the Space/project qualifies; no published renewal term. The old public application page (huggingface.co/community-grants) is reported dead as of 2026-Q2; the current channel is the per-Space Settings tab link / Community discussion.
  - *Gotchas:* Approval takes days and is discretionary ('see if yours makes the cut'); open public demos typically approved, commercial ones not; grant docs come mainly from the official huggingface/skills repo rather than a dedicated marketing page, so policy can shift without announcement; no guarantees on hardware size.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/huggingface/skills/blob/main/skills/huggingface-spaces/references/grants.md
- **2025–26 change:** Bottom line as of 2026-10-22: Hugging Face has NO live free offer specifically for students or startups on the Inference Providers front. (1) STARTUP PROGRAM — sunset: the former 'HF for Startups' program (widely reported as $1,000/mo of partner cloud credits for up to 12 months plus ~50% off the Enterprise Hub in year 1; original announcement page now 404) no longer appears on any HF-owned page — independently confirmed Sept 2026 (guptadeepak) and Oct 2026 (CloudRoute excluded HF from its verified program catalog). Third-party '6 months free PRO' claims are affiliate promo-code pages, unverified against official pages; huggingface.co/startups is an org profile, not a program page. (2) FREE ACCOUNT CREDIT — sunset: free accounts historically received a small monthly Inference Providers credit ($0.10/mo, 'subject to change'; TechCrunch 2025-01-28: 'all HF users get a small quota of credits'); the official pricing docs were updated 2026-10-07 to 'None' for free users, and only PRO ($9/mo, $2/mo credits) and Team/Enterprise ($2/seat/mo) receive included credits. (3) EDUCATION — no education license or student offer exists on any HF page today; an 'Academia Hub' org tier appears only in the rate-limits docs (no pricing, no application path, not free). (4) TRIAL CREDITS — HF publishes no trial-credit offer for Inference Providers (no signup bonus, no 30-day trial of PRO; a 2026 forum thread indicates paying for PRO with credits was also removed). Only genuinely free routes: the free Hub/Spaces tier (CPU Basic + ZeroGPU, 5 min/day, no card), the $0 custom-provider-key routing path (BYO provider key; provider's own free tiers apply), and 'community GPU grants for side projects' mentioned without a live application URL. No regional restrictions found; global. Sources: [Hugging Face Inference Providers pricing (official, read today)](https://huggingface.co/docs/inference-providers/en/pricing) · [Hugging Face docs hub rate limits](https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/en/rate-limits) · [Hugging Face docs hub billing](https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/billing) · [Hugging Face PRO](https://huggingface.co/pro) · [Hugging Face pricing](https://huggingface.co/pricing) · [Hugging Face enterprise](https://huggingface.co/enterprise) · [guptadeepak: HF Startup Program discontinued (Sep 2026 check)](https://guptadeepak.com/startup-offers/programs/huggingface-startups) · [CloudRoute startup-credits changelog Oct 2026](https://www.cloudroutehq.com/startup-credits/changes) · [Free LLM Credits changelog (HF free credit ended Oct 2026)](https://freellmcredits.com/changes) · [PerkBook: HF for Startups 'No Longer Available'](https://perkbook.co/startup-programs/hugging-for-startups/) · [SaaSTweaks: 6 months free PRO claim (affiliate, unverified)](https://saastweaks.com/startup-credits/hugging-face-pro-credits) · [TechCrunch: Inference Providers launch, Jan 2025](https://techcrunch.com/2025/01/28/hugging-face-makes-it-easier-for-devs-to-run-ai-models-on-third-party-clouds/) · [HF forum: paying for PRO with credits no longer possible](https://discuss.huggingface.co/t/is-it-not-possible-to-pay-for-pro-with-credits-anymore/173037). Note: web-search budget was exhausted mid-task; all confidence labels above reflect pages I fetched directly today unless flagged as third-party/unverified."
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 material changes: (1) Mid-2026 (docs PR merged 2026-07-21; forum reports from 2026-07-09): creating compute Spaces — Gradio or Docker, including formerly-free CPU Basic — now requires a paid plan (PRO $9/mo personal, Team/Enterprise for orgs); free personal accounts were pushed to 'Static only, or up to 2 ZeroGPU Spaces'. This materially shrank the free tier that previously let anyone run a free CPU Gradio Space. (2) 2026-06-29: ZeroGPU hosting formalized a free tier — 'accounts in good standing' (verified email, >30 days old) host up to 2 ZeroGPU Spaces; PRO up to 10; paid orgs up to 50. Before this the free hosting cap/wording changed repeatedly. (3) December 2025: H100 / 8x H100 removed from the Spaces hardware lineup. (4) ZeroGPU hardware migrated from A100 (2024 launch) to NVIDIA RTX Pro 6000 Blackwell by 2026; post-migration some workloads silently fall back to xlarge, doubling quota consumption (forum reports May 2026). (5) No student plan has ever shipped; HF staff confirmed in Oct 2025 that Academia Hub is the only education path. (6) The $10M GPU pledge (May 2024, ZeroGPU launch, via A100s) was a one-off commitment — the durable free offer is the ZeroGPU daily quota, not a $ pool. (7) Payment gotcha: PRO is credit-card-only; India-issued cards are not yet supported (use AWS/Google Cloud Marketplace billing workaround). Free account and all free-tier usage require no card. No region limits on free usage found.
- **2025–26 change:** No free education license or startup program: Hugging Face's education offering is Academia Hub, a PAID institutional plan ($10/seat/month, min 250 annual seats, incl. $2/seat/month compute credits and higher ZeroGPU priority) — institutions like ESCP (Aug 2025) pay for it; there is no self-serve .edu free program and no dedicated free startup program (no application form, no funding-cap criteria found; community GPU grants are the closest equivalent and accept 'Company project' cases). No free ML training/notebook compute exists: HF Jobs and Inference Endpoints are on-demand paid, and the only free inference-side compute is ZeroGPU's 5 min/day plus CPU Basic $0/hr. Material 2025-2026 changes: (1) new Gradio/Docker Spaces now require a paid plan (PRO personal / Team-Enterprise org) — previously CPU Spaces were free for all; free accounts retain only Static Spaces + up to 2 ZeroGPU Gradio Spaces (verified in the spaces-overview doc, 2026-10-22). (2) ZeroGPU hardware is now shared NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (48/96GB slices) replacing the earlier A100/A10G-based shared fleet; the original program (May 2024, ~$10M commitment, The Verge) survives in this form. (3) The former public community-grants application page (huggingface.co/community-grants) is reported 404 as of 2026-Q2; grants are now requested per-Space via the Settings tab link / Community discussion. (4) A May 2026 press item (CBW News) claims ~$5/month free GPU credits for all accounts; this does not appear on huggingface.co/pricing or the official docs and is unverified (low confidence). Free-account 100GB private storage, best-effort public storage, and 'protected' visibility gating to paid plans also confirmed 2026-10-22. WebSearch session budget was exhausted after 2 searches, so sunset/change claims rest on the official pages fetched today plus third-party changelogs (aifree.dev, perq.sh, The Verge, ESCP).
- **Critic correction** (Hugging Face Inference Providers): Contradicted by official docs. The Inference Providers pricing page (read Oct 2026) still gives Free users $0.10 of monthly credit (marked subject to change), PRO users $2.00 per month, and pay-as-you-go after that. The claim that free accounts get none is not supported. Oct 2026 forum threads report some new free accounts showing $0.00 balances, so availability looks inconsistent. Restate as reduced to $0.10/month, subject to change.
- **Critic correction** (outdated: creating Gradio or Docker Spaces now needs a paid plan): A September 2026 secondary source says creating Gradio or Docker Spaces needs PRO ($9 per month) or Team, while free accounts can still host two ZeroGPU Spaces. Update the free-tier wording and verify on the Hugging Face pricing page before republishing.

### Kaggle Notebooks

- **Kaggle Notebooks free GPU weekly quota (30 h/week, P100 or T4)** (high) — Official docs state Kaggle 'provides free access to NVIDIA TESLA P100 GPUs' and that the GPU quota 'resets weekly and is 30 hours or sometimes higher depending on demand and resources'. T4s (16 GB) were added as a GPU option alongside P100 in the 2023 notebooks update ('New GPU (T4s) options & more CPU RAM'). V100/A100 are not in the free pool. No credit card required; a free account suffices. Quota resets weekly (community tracking: Friday/Saturday midnight CET); the float above 30 h is not predictable. Interactive sessions have a 60-minute idle timeout (confirmed on the official GPU-usage tips page); batch (commit) sessions may run longer.
  - *Eligibility:* Any free Kaggle account (email/Google/Facebook sign-up). No .edu requirement, no CC. Phone verification (one-time SMS/voice call) gates accelerator access per secondary sources (medium confidence). Some regions have historically had restricted/intermittent accelerator availability (e.g., prior EU/UK data-sovereignty restrictions; exact 2026 state unverified — low confidence).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at kaggle.com -> create/open a notebook -> Settings tab -> Accelerator dropdown -> select 'GPU' (or 'GPU T4') -> session runs against the 30 h/week pool; remaining hours shown in notebook sidebar / kaggle.com/notebooks / settings. No claim steps needed — quota is automatic on every account.
  - *Expiry:* Quota never expires; resets weekly (historically Sat 00:00 UTC / Fri midnight CET). Per-session: 60 min idle timeout for interactive sessions.
  - *Gotchas:* Quota counts session wall-time with accelerator attached, not actual GPU utilization — a T4 session parked open burns hours. Quota 'sometimes higher' is not in advance and can differ by region/demand. Phone verification required to enable accelerator. Free storage: ~20 GB notebook temp + 200 GB user datasets (secondary source, medium confidence).
  - *Source:* https://www.kaggle.com/docs/efficient-gpu-usage
- **Kaggle Notebooks free TPU weekly quota (~20 h/week, v2/v3 8-core)** (medium) — Kaggle offers free 8-core TPU access (TPU v2/v3 '8' nodes; v5e/v5lite available on request but intermittent). Widely documented quota is 20 TPU hours/week for TPUs, with a 9-hour max per session (task's known-so-far figure matches this). The TPU docs page at kaggle.com/docs/tpus 404'd during this check, so the 20 h figure is from pre-2026 documentation/secondary sources rather than a re-verified official page — treat as medium confidence and re-verify in-browser.
  - *Eligibility:* Any free Kaggle account; TPU access (especially v5e/v5lite) may need an email request to Kaggle support; availability is intermittent and region-dependent. No credit card.
  - *How to claim:* Notebook -> Settings -> Accelerator dropdown -> 'TPU v2/v3 8-core' (or request v5e/v5lite). 20 h/week pool shared across notebook sessions; check remaining hours in settings/usage panel.
  - *Expiry:* Resets weekly with GPU quota; 9 h max per TPU session.
  - *Gotchas:* Quota is shared per-week with no carryover; 9 h session cap is shorter than some GPU batch sessions; TPU pool availability fluctuates — your run can be queued or no-accelerator if pool is full; idle timeout still applies to interactive sessions.
  - *Source:* https://www.kaggle.com/docs/notebooks(TPUlimitssection;/docs/tpusreturned404on2026-10-22)
- **Kaggle free CPU notebook time (~60 h/week)** (medium) — Notebooks also give a free weekly CPU allowance, commonly documented at 60 hours/week for 2-core / 13 GB RAM / 20 GB temp storage runs (secondary figure, not re-verified on an official page today — medium confidence). No CC required.
  - *Eligibility:* All free accounts; no phone verification typically needed for CPU-only sessions.
  - *How to claim:* Automatic on any account; select 'None' accelerator in notebook Settings.
  - *Expiry:* Resets weekly with the other pools.
  - *Gotchas:* CPU hours are tracked separately from GPU/TPU; 60 h figure has varied historically — verify in the in-app usage panel.
  - *Source:* https://www.kaggle.com/docs/notebooks
- **No separate Kaggle student or startup free-offer** (medium) — Kaggle does not run a distinct free student program, .edu-verified education license, or startup credit program for notebooks compute. Students and startups get the identical free Notebook quotas described above by virtue of a free account; Kaggle's education offering (courses, micro-credentials) is a separate thing and does not change compute quotas. If a startup-program search intended 'Kaggle startup credits', no such official program was found as of this check.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — no distinct offer to claim.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim; sign up free and use the general free quotas.
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse Kaggle with GitHub Student Pack-style programs; Kaggle has no age/funding-cap or .edu-gating for compute access.
  - *Source:* https://www.kaggle.com/docs/efficient-gpu-usage(nostudent/startupofferlisted)

### Lambda Research Grant

- **Lambda Research Grant (up to $5,000 GPU cloud credits for researchers)** (high) — Lambda Research Grant: up to $5,000 in Lambda cloud (GPU) credits for developing and showcasing research work on Lambda Instances.
