  - *How to claim:* 1) Join the free Developer Program. 2) Browse NGC catalog / build.nvidia.com model cards, pull NIM container (pull command + Helm chart shown under each model's Deploy tab). 3) Deploy on your own NVIDIA GPU(s), up to 16 for free dev/test.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to Developer Program membership; no published end date. Terms change with the NVIDIA Software License Agreement.
  - *Gotchas:* 16-GPU cap: commercial/production deployments are a license violation without AI Enterprise. You still pay for GPUs (cloud or hardware). This offer is the 2024-era framing from the official NIM FAQ; current self-host pricing is also bundled into the paid AI Enterprise product (from ~$4,500/GPU/yr).
  - *Source:* https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/nvidia-nim-faq/300317
- **NVIDIA AI Enterprise 90-day free trial license (self-hosted, production-grade)** (medium) — One-time 90-day evaluation license for the full NVIDIA AI Enterprise suite (NIM microservices, NeMo, Omniverse, K8s tooling; NVIDIA-hosted trial also mentions a free trial of NIM endpoints). Each evaluation license includes 128 concurrent users. Trial clock starts when the activation key is redeemed in the licensing portal (nvid.nvidia.com/dashboard). Renewal: no auto-renewal; a second 90-day eval license can be requested via a form, and longer POCs are referred to NVIDIA Partner Network partners.
  - *Eligibility:* Organizations with corporate/business email — personal emails (Google/Yahoo etc.) are rejected on application. Compatible infrastructure required (NVIDIA Certified Systems / product support matrix); individuals without NVIDIA hardware are steered to NVIDIA sales. No credit card or payment info visible in the registration flow; it is a license, not a bill.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/products/ai-enterprise/get-started/ → 'Request Free 90-Day License' (or the Test Drive redirect URL). 2) Register with a corporate email at enterpriseproductregistration.nvidia.com. 3) Wait ~24h for approval email with license-portal access. 4) Redeem the Product Activation Key, download software from NGC, run a license server.
  - *Expiry:* Exactly 90 days from activation-key redemption; one-time per org, extension via a re-application or partner POC. Trial includes Omniverse but NOT Run:ai (separate contact needed).
  - *Gotchas:* Personal/freemail addresses are auto-denied — students without an org email effectively cannot claim it, so the 90-day trial is an org/startup offer, not a student offer. 'Failed to acquire license' message = trial over. Production use of the free Developer-Program NIM endpoints remains disallowed even while holding a trial — the trial is for self-hosted eval on your GPUs.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/products/ai-enterprise/get-started/
- **NVIDIA Inception startup program (free membership, cloud credits, no equity)** (high) — Free global virtual program for AI startups: no application fee, no membership fee, no equity taken; 'no deadlines or cohorts' — apply anytime, review ~2-4 weeks. Members get: free cloud credits from NVIDIA and partners (NVIDIA publishes NO fixed dollar figure on the page; third-party 2026 guides cite up to $100,000 in NVIDIA DGX Cloud credits for H100 capacity and up to $100,000 AWS credits via AWS Activate, plus free NIM API endpoints for prototyping), free NVIDIA Deep Learning Institute self-paced courses + discounted workshops, preferred pricing on select NVIDIA hardware/software (rebate off supplier price), full Developer Forum access, VC introductions via Inception Capital Connect, co-marketing badges/events. A 30% DGX Cloud discount tier reportedly requires a 4-node / $75,000 minimum spend (funded teams).
  - *Eligibility:* Company must be: officially incorporated, less than 10 years old, with a clear use case for NVIDIA technology (AI/ML/CV/robotics/physical AI/simulation/HPCC), actively operated by at least one founder, with a working website. No revenue, no VC funding, no US incorporation required. Excluded: consulting/outsourced-dev firms, crypto-associated companies, cloud service providers, resellers/distributors, public companies. Students/individuals cannot apply (incorporated company required).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to nvidia.com/en-us/startups/ → Apply. 2) Sign in with an NVIDIA Developer account, fill company profile (name, site, founding date, team, focus area). 3) Describe the product in 3-4 sentences with explicit AI/GPU angle. 4) Submit; after review a partner manager activates benefits (credits, NIM, training) via intro call. Contact: inceptionprogram@nvidia.com.
  - *Expiry:* Membership is open-ended (no published end date; benefits 'for the duration of membership'). Credit expiry: NVIDIA does not publish on the official page — partner credit windows vary (AWS Activate typically 1-2 years).
  - *Gotchas:* No published dollar figure exists on NVIDIA's page for cloud credits — the $100K DGX Cloud and $100K AWS numbers come from third-party 2026 guides, so verify with your account manager before budgeting. Credits burn out while the ongoing GPU bill continues. No guaranteed access to scarce GPUs. Preferred pricing is a rebate, not a guarantee of supply.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/startups/
- **NVIDIA Innovation Lab / DGX Cloud Innovation Lab for Inception startups** (medium) — Select NVIDIA Inception members can apply for the NVIDIA Innovation Lab (a.k.a. DGX Cloud Innovation Lab): ~2 months of hands-on access to NVIDIA DGX Cloud infrastructure (H100-class GPUs), integrated AI software, pre-optimized environments, and direct NVIDIA expert support/benchmarking guidance. NVIDIA's own page claims member results 'up to 17x faster performance and 90% shorter optimization cycles'. Tier-based: entry Inception gets portal access; Premier/selected members get the Lab with 'larger allocation'.
  - *Eligibility:* Must already be an accepted NVIDIA Inception member (which itself requires: incorporated, <10 years old, working website, NVIDIA use case, one active founder; excludes crypto, resellers, cloud providers, public companies). 'Purpose-built for early-stage' members; selection is invitation/application-based, not automatic for all Inception members.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply to and be accepted into NVIDIA Inception (nvidia.com/en-us/startups). 2) Once in the member portal, apply for/complete the DGX Cloud Innovation Lab (selected members are invited; the FAQ says 'select members are invited to apply'). 3) Work with the assigned NVIDIA expert during the 2-month window.
  - *Expiry:* The Lab itself is a fixed ~2-month engagement per application; the parent Inception membership is open-ended. No published repeat-access policy.
  - *Gotchas:* It is a compute grant, not a perpetual free tier — after 2 months the same 30% discount / paid DGX Cloud pricing applies. Availability is discretionary ('select members'), so acceptance into Inception does not guarantee the Lab. No equity taken by NVIDIA either way.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/innovation-lab/
- **Free hosted NIM endpoints covered by the API Trial Terms (data/usage caveats for everyone, incl. students)** (high) — The formal NVIDIA API Trial Terms of Service (PDF, last modified 2025-10-07, read in full) govern all free build.nvidia.com inference: access is 'for limited trial purposes only and without use of the API Service or Generated Content in production' (§1.2). NVIDIA 'may extend trial service credits' (amounts per API service; §1.4) — after credits are exhausted, production requires a separate paid Subscription. §3.3: NVIDIA collects session metrics, error/execution logs, feedback, and User/Generated Content 'to improve NVIDIA products and services, including AI models', and usage is logged for security/fraud and shared with third-party providers. §2.4: fine-tuning service stores user content 30 days and generated content 90 days unless a subscription is purchased. §11.2: NVIDIA may terminate your use 'at any time without notice'.
  - *Eligibility:* Applies to every free-tier user (students, individual devs, startups alike); requires an adult of legal age in the country of use, Developer Program account + phone-verified API key, and a supported country (sanctioned-region exclusions apply). No .edu email, no CC, no funding cap.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim — it is the legal layer under the free tier above. Read the Trial Terms PDF linked from every build.nvidia.com model card ('The trial service is governed by the NVIDIA API Trial Terms of Service').
  - *Expiry:* No expiry of the terms themselves; each trial/credit grant, model endpoint, or service can be terminated by NVIDIA at any time (§11.2, §1.3 pre-release clauses). Arbitration/class-waiver opt-out must be sent within 30 days of starting use to be valid (§14.4).
  - *Gotchas:* Do not send PII, financial/health/government data or PCI data (§4.3). Do not use free endpoints to build a product that 'compete[s] with the API Service' (§4.12). No sublicensing or redistribution of outputs (§4.2). Because content can be used to improve NVIDIA models, treat the free tier as unsuitable for confidential workloads. 'Free' ≠ perpetual: endpoints and rate limits can change or be sunset at NVIDIA's discretion with no liability.
  - *Source:* https://assets.ngc.nvidia.com/products/api-catalog/legal/NVIDIA%20API%20Trial%20Terms%20of%20Service.pdf
- **NVIDIA Inception Program (startups) — free membership & core benefits** (high) — Free, no-equity program: free self-paced NVIDIA Deep Learning Institute courses, discounted expert-led workshops, free cloud credits from NVIDIA and partners, preferred pricing (rebate on select hardware/software), NVIDIA Developer Forums access, Inception Capital Connect (VC exposure), badges/co-marketing. No application fees, deadlines, or cohorts. Note: the task's entry URL nvidia.com/en-us/ai-catalog/inception/ now 404s; the live official page is nvidia.com/en-us/startups/ (verified today).
  - *Eligibility:* Company must be officially incorporated, less than 10 years old, actively operated by at least one founder, with a clear NVIDIA technology use case; any funding stage including bootstrapped; global, no stated region limit. Excluded: consulting/outsourced-dev firms, crypto-related companies, cloud service providers, resellers/distributors, public companies. NOT a .edu program — no student individual track, a legal entity is required.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at nvidia.com/en-us/startups/ (portal: programs.nvidia.com/phoenix/application). 2) Company is reviewed; keep profile/product details current in the portal to remain eligible. 3) Request benefits (cloud credits, partner offers, courses) from the Inception benefits catalog; partners approve individually. No credit card required for membership.
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry for membership; credits have partner-defined validity (e.g., Yotta 24 months, GCP 2 years).
  - *Gotchas:* 'Free cloud credits' is a per-benefit request, not a fixed grant — amounts depend on which partner benefit you request. Preferred pricing is a rebate via suppliers, not a discount NVIDIA can guarantee. Membership can lapse if the portal profile goes stale.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/startups/
- **NVIDIA DGX Cloud credits — up to $100,000 for H100 capacity (Inception members)** (medium) — Announced at GTC 2025 (March 2025 press release 'NVIDIA Expands Cloud Benefits for Startups'): NVIDIA offers qualified Inception members up to $100,000 in NVIDIA DGX Cloud credits for dedicated NVIDIA H100 GPU capacity, plus expert office hours. The figure still appears on NVIDIA's official Cloud Partners page as of today (2026-10-22), but the Inception main page does not list the benefit and 'qualified' criteria are unpublished; current availability not reconfirmable today.
  - *Eligibility:* Inception members (see base program: incorporated, <10 yrs old, any funding stage). 'Qualified members approved by NVIDIA after requesting the benefit in the portal' — qualification criteria are not public. No student path.
  - *How to claim:* As an Inception member, request the DGX Cloud credit benefit in the Inception benefits catalog; NVIDIA reviews and may approve up to $100,000. No CC required by NVIDIA itself.
  - *Expiry:* Not published.
  - *Gotchas:* Gated request, not automatic; no dollar amount promised at membership; criteria unpublished; if NVIDIA has wound this down, it was quietly — the Cloud Partners page still advertises it (stale-risk).
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/gpu-cloud-computing/partners/
- **NVIDIA Cloud Partner free credits via Inception — Nebius up to $150K (+$10K inference), Yotta up to $50K, plus Lambda/Scaleway/Lintasarta** (medium) — GTC 2025 (April 2025) expansion: Nebius 'AI Lift' — up to $150,000 in Nebius AI Cloud credits (NVIDIA GPUs) plus $10,000 specifically for inference workloads; first 3 months of the program also gave priority Blackwell access. Yotta — up to $50,000 in credits in three tiers ($10K/$15K/$25K), valid 24 months (confirmed on Yotta's official program page today). Lambda and Scaleway offer credits; NVIDIA said these partner/ISV offers total ~$300,000 in credits and discounts combined. Each partner sets its own amount/terms and approves the request.
  - *Eligibility:* NVIDIA Inception or NVIDIA Connect members who request the specific partner benefit in the Inception portal and are approved by that partner. Global; partner-specific terms apply (Yotta's $15K/$25K tiers need more detail about your NVIDIA-platform usage).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Join Inception. 2) In the benefits catalog, request the desired partner (Nebius/Yotta/etc.). 3) Partner reviews and issues credits on its platform; Yotta credits valid 24 months.
  - *Expiry:* Yotta: 24 months from grant; others partner-defined.
  - *Gotchas:* Amounts are partner-defined, not NVIDIA's; partner approvals can be slow. Yotta tiers require describing how you use NVIDIA SDKs/platform. The $300K 'total' NVIDIA headline is a marketing aggregate, not stackable credits.
  - *Source:* https://yotta.com/NVIDIA-Startup-Inception-Program/Program-Structure.html
- **AWS Activate via Inception (NVIDIA-issued Org ID) — Portfolio up to $200,000; self-funded Founders up to $5,000; invite-only AI tier $200,000+** (high) — Official AWS Activate page (verified today): Activate Portfolio = up to $200,000 in credits (pre-Series B, requires Org ID from an Activate provider; NVIDIA is an approved provider via Inception). Activate Founders (self-funded/bootstrapped) = start with $1,000, up to $5,000 total, apply directly without Org ID. New 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' = $200,000+, invite-only for scaling startups via account manager. A new 'Kiro Startup Credits' track also appears. Credits are redeemable on third-party models on Amazon Bedrock; 'AWS Account on Paid Tier Plan' is required. NVIDIA developer forums (2025–2026) show members previously receiving $10K/$25K tiers and up to $75K–$100K Scale-tier approvals, plus persistent Org-ID handoff issues between Inception and AWS.
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 10 years; Pre-Series B (Portfolio); most recent funding round rules apply on AWS's side; bootstrapped founders use the Founders tier directly; for Inception-referred applicants: Inception membership + NVIDIA-confirmed AWS benefit + NVIDIA Org ID. AWS paid-tier account (credit card on file) required.
  - *How to claim:* Inception route: 1) Join Inception, request the AWS benefit in the portal. 2) NVIDIA confirms eligibility and issues an AWS Activate Org ID. 3) Apply on AWS Activate (startups.aws.com) using the Org ID; AWS reviews (5–10 business days per AWS; community reports months). Direct route (self-funded): apply directly to Activate Founders, no Org ID. No .edu/student track (individuals just need an AWS account; students have AWS Free Tier separately).
  - *Expiry:* Activate credits validity is set by AWS promotional terms (typically 12 months from grant) — not restated on the page today.
  - *Gotchas:* Official tiers were restructured — the older member-reported $10K/$25K/Scale-up-to-$100K offers no longer appear on AWS's current page; the handoff (NVIDIA Org ID → AWS) has documented 3–15 month delays and auto-rejections ('already received credits at same or greater amount', 'provider has not confirmed affiliation'). Paid-tier account = CC required. Bedrock third-party model usage consumes credits.
  - *Source:* https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/
- **Google Cloud for Startups Cloud Program — up to $350,000 credits over 2 years (AI track)** (high) — Official GCP page (verified today): 'Up to $350,000 in Cloud credits over two years', plus credits for frontier models (Gemini/Gemma), technical/product training. Tier structure (footnotes on the page): AI startups — Year 1: up to $250,000 at 100%; Year 2: up to an additional $100,000 at 20%. Other startups — Year 1: up to $100,000 at 100%; Year 2: up to an additional $100,000 at 20%. 'Most tiers require verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding.' Third-party models are billed directly, not covered by credits. This program is a stated Inception partner benefit (NVIDIA↔GCP collab since April 2024 offered the up-to-$350K path through Inception).
  - *Eligibility:* Incorporated startup, verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding for most tiers; AI track requires the product to be AI-focused; apply at cloud.google.com/startup/apply. Inception members get an accelerated onboarding path (partner deal). No .edu requirement; not open to individuals/students. Card policy not restated on the page today (GCP account required; standard GCP trials normally take a card on file).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at cloud.google.com/startups (Apply to the program). 2) Verify funding stage / company details. 3) If Inception member, use the partner fast-track. 4) Draw down: AI — up to $250K year 1; +up to $100K at 20% (i.e., up to $20K effective in year 2 per footnote wording). 5) Credits cover Google-built models; third-party model inference is billed separately.
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable over 2 years as structured above.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000' means only 20% of year-2 usage up to $100K is credited, not the full amount — real max over 2 years is less than a naive $350K + $100K read. Third-party (e.g., non-Google) model API calls are excluded. Pre-Seed–Series A gate excludes bootstrapped/late-stage companies (unlike AWS's pre-Series B).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startups
- **Google Cloud free trial + always-free tier — $300 / new customers (open to students & individuals)** (high) — Official page (verified today): 'New customers get $300 in free credit to try Google Cloud products and build a proof of concept. You won't be charged until you activate your full paid account.' Plus 20+ always-free products with monthly limits ('$300 in free credits and 20+ free products'), free trial instances for GKE, Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB, Bigtable, plus Gemini Enterprise Plus free trial. This is the individual/student-reachable free GPU/cloud front of the ecosystem (GCP A-series GPU instances can be run on the $300 trial).
  - *Eligibility:* New GCP customers; open to anyone with a Google account (students with .edu or personal both qualify); no funding/age requirements; global. Standard signup takes a credit/debit card on file (not restated in today's page text — historical standard).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at console.cloud.google.com/freetrial; $300 credit is valid for 90 days under standard terms; always-free quotas apply regardless. Use on A3/A100/A100M GPU instances or Vertex AI for ML training jobs.
  - *Expiry:* $300 trial: 90 days (standard terms); always-free tier: indefinite, monthly limits.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial is a 90-day time-boxed trial, not perpetual free compute; if the paid account activates after trial expiry, standard pay-as-you-go applies; card required (verify current signup). GPU availability for A100/A3 is region-limited.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Microsoft for Startups — 'up to $150,000 in credits' on Azure + free AI tools & GitHub credits** (medium) — Official hub (verified today): 'Access cutting-edge AI models, developer tools, and up to $150,000 in credits' — a large 2025/2026 expansion vs the legacy $1,200/year Azure + $1,200 GitHub framing. Credits apply to eligible Azure services on a sponsored subscription; Microsoft Learn (today) documents credit-aware use across GitHub (Copilot, Actions, billing connected via the Azure subscription), Microsoft Foundry models, Fireworks models, and AKS (AKS usage on a sponsored subscription is auto-covered). Tier details and eligibility are gated behind the application flow, not published on the marketing page.
  - *Eligibility:* Incorporated startups; Microsoft's own 'Microsoft for Startups' application decides tier/amount; Inception members can apply independently (no NVIDIA Org ID involved). No .edu/student track — it is a startup program. Exact funding/age caps not stated on the pages verified today.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply via Microsoft for Startups (www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups → apply/claim benefits). 2) Claim 'Startup credits' into a sponsored Azure subscription. 3) Optionally connect that subscription as GitHub's payment method so GitHub Copilot/Actions metered usage bills against the credits. No CC required for the sponsored credits themselves (standard Azure account still needed).
  - *Expiry:* Not published on verified pages; Microsoft historically used 12-month renewal windows for Azure startup credits.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Up to $150,000' is a marketing ceiling — the bulk of recipients get far less; published tier table not found on marketing pages. Partner/community models on Foundry are billed separately from Microsoft-sold models. Legacy $1,200 figure no longer appears — check the application flow for what you will actually be offered.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/
- **NVIDIA Inception Innovation Lab — 60 days of hands-on NVIDIA GPU access (selected members)** (medium) — Inception benefit: 60 days of hands-on NVIDIA GPU access on a self-serve platform with pre-optimized environments and expert support, run in cohorts; NVIDIA provides the cloud instance (no dollar value published). Reported in the current (2026) Inception benefits summary; it is a selected-member benefit, not automatic.
  - *Eligibility:* Selected NVIDIA Inception members (cohort-based); same base Inception eligibility (incorporated, <10 yrs, NVIDIA use case).
  - *How to claim:* Join Inception, then be selected into an Innovation Lab cohort; request via the benefits catalog.
  - *Expiry:* 60 days per cohort.
  - *Gotchas:* Cohort selection — approval not guaranteed; time-boxed 60 days; no published dollar value; availability fluctuates.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudroutehq.com/startup-credits/nvidia-inception
- **build.nvidia.com — free NIM inference for open models incl. Nemotron (open access, incl. students)** (medium) — Official build.nvidia.com (verified today): 'Free inference with leading models' — free API endpoints for Nemotron models (and other open models, e.g., DeepSeek) plus pre-built agentic capabilities and blueprints. Open sign-up (no startup status or .edu stated as a requirement), so this is the individual/student-reachable 'free ML compute' front, but it is inference API, not GPU training or notebooks. Community reports a default RPM cap around 40; Inception members can request higher RPM for research workloads (forum, Oct 2026).
  - *Eligibility:* Any NVIDIA developer account; Inception members may request raised rate limits. No funding/institution gate documented. No CC stated for inference use.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign in at build.nvidia.com. 2) Use the model's API endpoint / key to run inference free within rate limits. 3) Inception members: request higher RPM via the Inception portal / developer forums for research benchmarks.
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry; free tier subject to change.
  - *Gotchas:* Inference-only — cannot train or host notebooks here; rate-limited; free-tier availability of specific models can change; heavier workloads require paid or partner-cloud (Inception credit) routes.
  - *Source:* https://build.nvidia.com/
- **NVIDIA education angle — free self-paced DLI courses; no dedicated student GPU-compute program found** (medium) — NVIDIA's student/education-free offer is educational, not compute: free self-paced Deep Learning Institute courses (course codes distributed via Inception membership; public DLI catalog is also free), discounted instructor-led workshops, free CUDA Toolkit/NGC developer downloads. No NVIDIA 'education license' or .edu GPU quota program (e.g., Colab-style notebooks) was found in the Inception or NVIDIA education pages as of today; student GPU compute in this ecosystem effectively comes through the partner free trials (GCP $300/90-day + free A-series eligibility, AWS Free Tier, Azure) or via starting a company and joining Inception.
  - *Eligibility:* Individuals/students: free DLI courses, build.nvidia.com inference, partner free trials (no .edu email specifically required by NVIDIA; .edu mainly matters at third parties like Google's student pricing elsewhere). Startups: Inception (incorporated entity).
  - *How to claim:* Students: enroll in DLI courses / use build.nvidia.com free inference / claim GCP $300 trial and AWS Free Tier on a personal account. Startups: apply to Inception for the partner-credit stack above.
  - *Expiry:* N/A (courses free; trials per partner terms).
  - *Gotchas:* Do not conflate 'free trials' (time-boxed, card on file, pay-as-you-go after) with 'free' — only DLI, build.nvidia.com inference (rate-limited), and partner credits are genuinely free.
  - *Source:* https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/startups/
- **2025–26 change:** Education/student-specific offers: as of 2026-10-22 I could NOT verify a dedicated NVIDIA education license or student program for inference/AI Enterprise (NVIDIA education-related URLs www.nvidia.com/en-us/education/ and /about-nvidia/education/ return 404; my web-search budget for this session was exhausted, so I could not confirm whether a GTC-era 'AI for Students' pilot at partner universities still exists). What is verifiably available to students is: the free Developer Program NIM tier (finding 1) and the free 16-GPU self-hosted NIM license (finding 2) — both open to individual students with no .edu requirement, plus free DLI courses if their startup is in Inception. A dedicated student free-offer line could not be confirmed and should not be cited. Sunset/changes 2025-2026: (a) The legacy '1,000 signup credits, expandable to 5,000 with work-email verification' mechanism reported on the NIM forum in 2024-2025 has effectively been replaced by the published 40 RPM + 10,000 requests/day rate-limit model — official model pages show no credit counter; 2026-08 forum threads still use the old framing, NVIDIA has not officially confirmed the credits pool was removed (disputed; check account panel). (b) The 2024 official NIM FAQ's framing ('160+ models', '90-day trial' linked off the dev-program page) is superseded by the 2026 catalog rotation (Nemotron 3.x/DeepSeek V4.x/GLM 5.3 generation) and by consolidation of Omniverse terms into 'NVIDIA AI Enterprise' (AI Product Terms last modified 2026-04-15). (c) DGX Cloud's old 'free trial' web page (nvidia.com/.../free-trial-nvidia-ai-enterprise) now 301-redirects to the corporate-email enterprise registration portal, meaning the self-serve free trial path has been tightened to organizations with corporate emails. (d) Rate limits are explicitly non-adjustable on the free tier; moderators direct all RPM-increase requests to paid AI Enterprise channels (2026 forum replies). Inception publishes no dollar figure for its cloud credits; widely-cited 'up to $100,000 DGX Cloud' number is third-party-sourced only. Regional note: the free tier is unavailable in Russia, Belarus, Iran, Kazakhstan, Uzbekistan, Kyrgyzstan, Pakistan, Bangladesh and 6 other countries (NVIDIA's own otp-unsupported-countries config, read 2026-09-26).
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes/sunsets: (1) The task's entry URL https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/ai-catalog/inception/ now returns 404 — Inception's live official page moved to https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/startups/ (verified today). (2) AWS Activate restructured: the member-reported Inception-ecosystem tiers ($10K base / $25K Grow / up to $100K Scale, per NVIDIA developer-forum threads 2024-2025) no longer appear on AWS's current official page; the official structure is now Activate Founders (self-funded, up to $5,000, start $1,000), Activate Portfolio (pre-Series B, up to $200,000, NVIDIA Org ID via Inception), and invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+), plus a new 'Kiro Startup Credits' track. Documented 3-15 month handoff delays (Org ID issues) persist in late-2025/2026 forum threads. (3) GCP for Startups is still up to $350K, now formally structured over 2 years (Year-1 100% / Year-2 20% footnotes, up to $250K Y1 AI / $100K Y1 others) with verifiable Pre-Seed to Series A funding required for most tiers — the older flat 'up to $200,000' marketing was effectively superseded by the $350K AI headline. The $300/90-day GCP free trial is unchanged. (4) Microsoft for Startups headline credits expanded from the legacy $1,200/yr Azure + $1,200 GitHub to 'up to $150,000 in credits' with Foundry/GitHub credit-aware usage (current official hub). (5) New since 2025: NVIDIA DGX Cloud H100 credit cap (up to $100,000, GTC 2025) and Nebius AI Lift (up to $150K + $10K inference, GTC April 2025). Nothing on the GCP $300 trial, GCP always-free tier, or build.nvidia.com free inference was sunset; Microsoft and AWS program details not published beyond the levels quoted. No confirmed full sunset of any offer in this stack as of 2026-10-22, though the $100K DGX Cloud offer's current operational availability is unconfirmable (still advertised on NVIDIA's Cloud Partners page but absent from the main Inception page).

### Ollama

- **Ollama local inference runtime (free, open source)** (high) — Download Ollama's desktop app or CLI and run open-weight models (Llama, Qwen, Mistral, Gemma, gpt-oss, DeepSeek, etc.) on your own CPU/GPU at $0. No account, no API key, no per-token billing; local usage is unlimited and it works fully offline / air-gapped. Permissive open source: the repo LICENSE file is MIT (the 'known so far' note said Apache-2.0 — that is outdated). Scale per ollama.com homepage: 9M+ monthly installs, 1B+ model downloads, 200T+ tokens served. This is the core 'free compute' angle for students/startups: you pay for hardware, not for the runtime.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, worldwide — no .edu email, no startup age/funding cap, no credit card, no region limits. Identical for students and startups as for everyone else.
  - *How to claim:* Download from ollama.com (desktop app or CLI), install, then `ollama run <model>` (e.g. `ollama run llama3`). Models pull from ollama.com's registry. No signup required for local use.
  - *Expiry:* None — permanent, no time limit or renewal, for as long as Ollama is maintained.
  - *Gotchas:* You pay for your own hardware (RAM/VRAM + electricity + depreciation) — that is the real cost surface; no free GPU or free training compute is bundled. Ollama is an inference/serving runtime, not a notebook/training platform; fine-tuning needs separate tooling. License is MIT, not Apache-2.0. No egress/egress-style fees locally (data stays on your machine).
  - *Source:* https://ollama.com
- **Ollama Cloud Free tier ($0 hosted GPU inference)** (high) — Ollama Cloud's Free plan is $0/mo and includes a small 'Starter usage credits' allowance (a modest monthly usage pool for a set of 'starter models'; no dollar figure is published). 1 concurrent request. Zero data retention, hosted in the US/Europe (some Qwen models route to Singapore); prompts are never logged or trained on. No service fees, no subscription. Model pay-as-you-go rates range ~$0.015/M cached-input (nemotron-3-super) up to $15/M output (kimi-k3); off-peak half-pricing applies on some models outside 12:00–18:00 UTC weekdays and all weekend. Free tier structure (monthly starter credits, no 5-hr/weekly caps) effective Aug 31, 2026.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone who creates a free account at ollama.com — no .edu, no startup age/funding cap, no region gate. Credit card is NOT required for the free tier; a payment method is only needed if you opt in to add pay-as-you-go credits to unlock all models.
  - *How to claim:* Create a free Ollama account at ollama.com → in the CLI run `ollama signin` → run a cloud model (e.g. `ollama run qwen3-coder:480b-cloud`, `gpt-oss:120b-cloud`) or call Ollama's OpenAI-compatible API. Starter usage is included; add credits to go beyond it.
  - *Expiry:* Free tier has no expiry; starter credits refresh monthly. No time limit on the $0 tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Starter-credit amount is not published (no dollar figure). Concurrency capped at 1; requests beyond are queued (a full queue rejects new requests). Overage = pay-as-you-go at published per-token rates (real cost). It is inference only — no free notebooks, no training GPU. This is a permanent free tier, not a trial/credit drop.
  - *Source:* https://ollama.com/pricing
- **No Ollama startup program, student/education discount, or nonprofit offer** (high) — As of 2026-10-22 Ollama offers NO dedicated startup program, NO student/academic discount, and NO nonprofit tier. ollama.com/pricing lists only Free / Pro / Max / Team / Enterprise — no discounted or special student/startup/edu/nonprofit plan. Discount aggregators (costbench, aitoolsatlas) print generic 'startup/education/nonprofit program' templates but explicitly state no Ollama-specific terms are verified. A third-party 2026-09-04 review confirms: 'Ollama has no student or academic discount' — the pitch is that the core software is already free and unrestricted, so there is nothing to discount.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — none of these programs exist. The only free offers available to students/startups are the general ones above (local runtime + Cloud Free tier), which need no special eligibility.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim; use the general Free tier / local runtime.
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* Treat auto-generated 'Ollama student discount / startup program / nonprofit discount' pages (costbench, aitoolsatlas, nozz's paid Founding-Member upsell, trytrick) as unverified, not official Ollama programs; confirm anything on ollama.com before relying on it.
  - *Source:* https://ollama.com/pricing
- **2025–26 change:** LICENSE CORRECTION: the 'known so far' note said Ollama is Apache-2.0 — the repo's LICENSE file is actually MIT (permissive open source; free and unrestricted). MATERIAL PRICING CHANGE: Ollama Cloud pricing was overhauled on Aug 31, 2026 to transparent monthly usage-credit pools. Legacy mechanics — 5-hour session limits + 7-day weekly caps, and the 'Pro = 50x Free / Max = 5x Pro' framing — are gone; new structure: Free (starter credits, 1 concurrent) / Pro $20/mo ($200/yr ≈ $16.67/mo, $60/mo credits, 3 concurrent) / Max $100/mo ($300/mo credits, 10 concurrent, early access to newest models) / Team $500/mo (early access, $1,000/mo shared credits, unlimited users) / Enterprise (custom, volume pricing). Cloud (hosted inference) models first launched in preview Sep 19, 2025; Team plan opened Aug 31, 2026. NOTHING FREE WAS SUNSET: the free local runtime and the Cloud Free tier remain $0 — only the free tier's usage-limit mechanism changed (session/weekly → monthly starter credits). ADJACENT / THIRD-PARTY 'free GPU + Ollama' offers that appear in search are NOT Ollama's own programs: UCloud CompShare (10 hours free RTX40-series GPU after signup + ID verification; 5% discount + compute subsidy for academic/enterprise certification; hosts Ollama images) and Brown CCV 'Oscar' HPC (800+ NVIDIA GPUs, Ollama preinstalled as a module, 100+ shared open models, free to Brown accounts). Ollama provides no free notebooks or free ML-training compute — its cloud is inference-only and the local runtime is for inference/serving; training/fine-tuning requires separate tooling.

### OVHcloud Free Trial

- **Public Cloud Free Trial (global, www.ovhcloud.com) — US$200 / 1 month** (high) — US$200 free trial credit (displayed in local currency of the account's market/country, ex-VAT) valid for one month, activated when the first Public Cloud project is created. Applies to any Public Cloud service in the catalogue across all regions where Public Cloud is offered, incl. GPU/AI: the page's worked example covers 35 hours of AI1-1-GPU AI Notebook and 5 hours of AI Training on 4 AI1-1-GPU nodes. One voucher per natural or legal person (even across multiple NIC handles). Non-refundable, non-transferable, no combining with other offers; if the credit outlives on another NIC handle OVHcloud may terminate services; amounts covered by the voucher are excluded from SLA calculation. Offer live since 30 Jun 2022.
  - *Eligibility:* Any OVHcloud customer (new or existing) who has NEVER created a Public Cloud project (active or past). A valid payment method (credit card/bank) must already be saved in the account before starting — a card IS required, contrary to some listings. No .edu/education email requirement. Available in all regions where Public Cloud is offered. One per person/entity.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create/log in to an OVHcloud account (ovhcloud.com). 2) Ensure a payment method is saved. 3) In the Control Panel open Public Cloud and create your first project — the FREETRIAL voucher activates on project creation. 4) Deploy resources; the US$200 is auto-deducted from that project's bills for one month. 5) After the month or when the credit runs out, the saved payment method is billed for further usage.
  - *Expiry:* Voucher valid 1 month from project activation; non-renewable, non-exchangeable. After expiry the account reverts to standard pay-as-you-go billing.
  - *Gotchas:* It's a trial credit, not a free tier — payment method required and unused credit vanishes after 30 days. VPS and bare-metal/dedicated servers are NOT covered (Public Cloud only); the trial page's own examples show GPU AI notebooks/AI training. Third-party trackers (agentdeals.dev) recorded a terms tightening on 2026-04-01 and a 2026-10-01 price change (Gen3 B3/C3/R3 instances now bill local storage and a public IPv4 as separate lines, raising total bills 1.4–21.9% for small instances), which makes the US$200 burn faster on Gen3 instances.
  - *Source:* https://www.ovhcloud.com/en/public-cloud/free-trial/
- **Public Cloud Free Trial (OVHcloud US, us.ovhcloud.com) — up to $300 / 90 days** (high) — US$200 credit when creating the first US Public Cloud project PLUS an additional $100 credit for creating the first Managed Database service — up to $300 total. Credits valid 90 days from the date they are applied (applied automatically; operational target 2 business days, contractual outer bound 30 days after OVHcloud receives the required $0.99 purchase). Cover Public Cloud only: compute (VMs, Cloud GPU, Local Zones), Block/Object/File storage, network, Managed Kubernetes, managed databases (MySQL, PostgreSQL, MongoDB, Valkey, Kafka, OpenSearch, Grafana), AI & ML (AI Notebooks, AI Training, AI Deploy) and security/ops services. Do NOT cover bare metal, VPS or Hosted Private Cloud.
  - *Eligibility:* US datacenters only; customers who have not previously purchased Public Cloud services from OVHcloud. Must purchase at least $0.99 of Public Cloud services to trigger; a payment method with a $0.99 fee must be added when creating the first project — i.e. a card is required (though effectively cost-free via the $0.99 charge). No promo code, no .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up for an OVHcloud US account (us.ovhcloud.com). 2) Create your first Public Cloud project in the Control Panel — add a payment method ($0.99 fee applies). 3) Launch your first resource ($0.99 purchase triggers eligibility). 4) The $200 credit auto-applies within 2 business days; buy your first Managed Database service to unlock the extra $100. 5) Check balance in the account's Payment Methods > vouchers tab.
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 90 days after being applied to the account. No renewal/extension; unusable balance is void. After expiry the account bills at standard rates via the default payment method.
  - *Gotchas:* The page's header says 'one-month free trial' but the terms state 90-day validity — treat 90 days as contractual. Charges continue automatically once credits are exhausted/expired via the card on file. US-only infrastructure: US regions with Public Cloud availability qualify. The $100 Managed-DB bonus is the part most easily missed.
  - *Source:* https://us.ovhcloud.com/public-cloud/free-trial/
- **OVHcloud Startup Program — Start level: €10,000 credits / 12 months (EU; $12,000 US)** (high) — 12-month membership: €10,000 in free cloud credits (US program: up to $12,000; Canada site shows $14,000) usable on Public Cloud and Hosted Private Cloud, plus 6 hours of free 1-on-1 engineering consultation, regional manager access, free social visibility ('Shine On'), Showcase events with VCs, mentoring. Credits are vouchers valid 12 months; unused vouchers are lost at the end. Voucher-eligible products: Public Cloud compute (instances incl. GPUs — only V100, V100s and RTX5000 GPU instances, all other GPUs excluded), Block/Object/Cold Archive storage, Managed K8s, managed databases, AI & ML (AI Notebooks, AI Training, AI Deploy, AI Endpoints), Quantum Notebooks; Hosted Private Cloud VMware/Nutanix. Not eligible: VPS, dedicated/bare-metal servers, Web Cloud, telecom. Also: quantum emulators get 'free credits via the Startup Program or free trial offer' per the QPU product page. No equity taken, no exclusivity (competing programs allowed).
  - *Eligibility:* Selected pre-seed and seed startups with at least a Proof of Concept or MVP deployable on OVHcloud, and a professional startup/business activity. Excluded: NGOs, associations, resellers/integrators, crypto mining, black-hat SEO, gaming bots, companies that already joined the program (incl. former Digital Launch Pad). No published company-age cap and no published funding cap for Start (OVHcloud decides the tier; any prior funding round 'improves chances' but isn't required; pre-revenue and revenue-generating both apply). Geographic: apply on the country site matching your business address; US infrastructure companies use the separate US program with a US account; generally one program membership (country of incorporation/operation). No .edu email; individual students aren't the target — it's company-level.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Pick the right country site at startup.ovhcloud.com (or us.ovhcloud.com/startup-program for US). 2) Click Apply and link an existing OVHcloud customer account or create a new one (use a business account, not personal). 3) Answer company questions; you may get an email requesting more info. 4) Decision within ~7 days. 5) On acceptance sign the program contract in the Control Panel; vouchers arrive within ~48 hours; a regional program manager onboards you. Spend vouchers on eligible services during the 12 months.
  - *Expiry:* Vouchers must be used within 12 months of the contract; remaining balance is automatically invalidated. No renewal; one membership per company, ever (re-applying after exit not allowed).
  - *Gotchas:* Credits can't pay for VPS or dedicated servers — the 'fronts: VPS' angle only works through Public Cloud instances/Bare Metal Cloud (Bare Metal Cloud IS listed eligible in OVH docs' eligible-products table, while classic 'Dedicated Servers' and 'VPS' are not). GPU access under the program is limited to V100/V100s/RTX5000 instances. Trial-vs-free: this is a selective, application-based credit grant, not self-serve. Application must be honest — false/vague info gets it rejected. If you order non-eligible products by mistake they bill your regular payment method.
  - *Source:* https://startup.ovhcloud.com/en/startups/
- **OVHcloud Startup Program — Scale level: up to €100,000 credits / 12 months (EU; up to $120,000 US)** (medium) — 12 months for Series A (and beyond) funded startups/scaleups: vouchers sized by OVHcloud's assessment of your expected 12-month infrastructure spend, capped at €100,000 (US: up to $120,000), plus up to 20 hours of 1-on-1 engineer time, Business-tier support, personalized quotes via Sales, personal visibility and market/funding event access. Same voucher eligibility rules as Start level (Public Cloud + Hosted Private Cloud; GPU only V100/V100s/RTX5000; no VPS/dedicated servers; AI Notebooks/Training/Deploy and Quantum Notebooks eligible). 'Enabler' route: accelerators/incubators/VCs that are OVHcloud partners can bring members to the same ceilings (up to €100k/20h); an event code is only mandatory when the support structure is a Premium partner.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups/scaleups that have raised Series A or later (funding stage is the main discriminator vs Start; OVHcloud makes the final tier decision). Same exclusions as Start level (NGOs, non-business projects, resellers, contentious activities, prior program members). Company site matching business address; US program requires a US OVHcloud account.
  - *How to claim:* Same as Start level: apply online at startup.ovhcloud.com (country site per your address) or us.ovhcloud.com/startup-program, link/create a business OVHcloud account, respond to follow-up, ~7-day decision, sign contract in Control Panel, vouchers within ~48h, manager onboarding.
  - *Expiry:* Vouchers valid 12 months from contract; unused balance lost. Participation capped at 12 months per level.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Up to' — actual amount is set by OVHcloud from your projected spend, not guaranteed at the cap. Series A+ requirement means most seed-stage teams get the €10k Start level instead. No exclusivity with other cloud providers' programs. GPU instances other than V100/V100s/RTX5000 aren't voucher-eligible even at Scale level.
  - *Source:* https://startup.ovhcloud.com/en/scaleups/
- **Public VCF-as-a-Service first month free (US promo, code VCFTRIAL)** (medium) — Limited-time promotion on OVHcloud US homepage: first month of Public VCF-as-a-Service (VMware Cloud Foundation workloads on shared infrastructure) free for new OVHcloud customers, using code VCFTRIAL at checkout. Standard Starter Pack only. This is a VMware-hosted private-cloud offering, not a GPU/VPS/dedicated-server product.
  - *Eligibility:* New OVHcloud US customers only; first 50 customers or until October 31, 2026, whichever comes first (i.e. it ends ~9 days after the 2026-10-22 reference date). No .edu requirement; payment at checkout after the free month.
  - *How to claim:* 1) New OVHcloud US account. 2) Order Public VCF-as-a-Service Standard Starter Pack. 3) Enter code VCFTRIAL at checkout to waive the first month.
  - *Expiry:* Offer limited to first 50 customers or 31 Oct 2026. After the free month, standard pricing auto-renews unless cancelled before the promotional period ends.
  - *Gotchas:* Auto-renews to paid — cancel if you don't want it. Strict customer cap (50). The promo is US-only and VMware-specific; unrelated to the $200/$300 Public Cloud trial or the Startup Program (don't assume it can be stacked — page says 'additional terms apply').
  - *Source:* https://us.ovhcloud.com/
- **Student/education offer: free access to OVHcloud quantum notebooks (Qualifying Students program)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Announced with the Quandela MosaiQ QPU inauguration (2024; rooted in the 2023 C12 notebook release): selected students from higher-education/research institutions (incl. QuanTEdu-France consortium members such as Telecom Paris) receive free access to OVHcloud's full quantum notebook range (Alice & Bob, C12, Eviden, Pasqal, Quandela, later Qiskit) and to the Quandela QPU via the cloud — original press announced 80 hours of notebook access per selected student. OVHcloud's current QPU product page says free emulation credits are 'available... particularly for startups, researchers or innovative companies — via the OVHcloud Startup Program or the free trial offer', i.e. students effectively ride the Startup Program/trial credits rather than a dedicated self-serve .edu offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Students at qualifying higher-education/research institutions; selection by application review (2024 program cited QuanTEdu-France/Telecom Paris). No .edu self-serve tier is published on ovhcloud.com today. Adjacent non-free education channel: OCRE 2024 framework (awarded to OVHcloud by GÉANT, 5-year agreement running since Feb 2025) gives research/education institutions in 9 European countries (BE, FR, DE, IT, NL, PL, PT, ES, UK) simplified procurement access to OVHcloud Public Cloud — negotiated framework terms, not free credits.
  - *How to claim:* No self-serve claim path published. Route: get your student/employer enrolled as an OVHcloud customer (e.g. via a startup/researcher account or OCRE-eligible institution) and use the US$200 Public Cloud free trial or apply to the Startup Program; request access to quantum notebooks/emulators in that account.
  - *Expiry:* No published expiry; the 2024 '80 hours' figure was per selected student. OCRE 2024 framework runs ~5 years from Feb 2025.
  - *Gotchas:* Low confidence this is still actively recruiting individual students — it was an announced ecosystem initiative, not advertised on current ovhcloud.com marketing pages as of 2026-10-22. Quantum notebook access is metered per-minute like other AI notebooks unless covered by trial/program credits. Don't count on a free .edu tier for VPS/dedicated servers — none exists.
  - *Source:* https://corporate.ovhcloud.com/en/newsroom/news/inauguration-quantum-computer/
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22: (1) The legacy offer in the task's 'known so far' — 'US$200 free trial on new dedicated servers & VPS, 2-month, no commitment (refundable)' — is GONE: https://www.ovhcloud.com/en/free-trial/ returns 404; the only live trial is the Public Cloud US$200/1-month trial (and up to $300/90 days in the US). No dedicated-server or VPS trial credit is advertised anywhere on ovhcloud.com or us.ovhcloud.com. (2) 2026-04-01: third-party changelog (agentdeals.dev) records OVHcloud narrowing the free-trial terms; and on 2026-10-01 OVHcloud restructured Gen3 Public Cloud instance pricing — local storage and public IPv4 on B3/C3/R3 are now billed as separate lines (~€109/TB/mo storage, ~$1.97/mo IPv4), raising bills 1.4–21.9% (largest for small instances), which consumes trial credits faster. (3) VPS pricing: VPS 2026 list prices were raised 36–49% effective 2026-04-01 (e.g. VPS-1 €4.49→€6.49/mo); the new 'VPS 2027' range launched (VPS-1 2027 from ~$4.54/mo per www.ovhcloud.com/en/vps/). No VPS free tier/trial exists. (4) Bare metal: Kimsufi budget range was discontinued and replaced by the 'Eco' range (~$30/mo, us.ovhcloud.com/deals); bare-metal prices rose 9–11% for new deployments post-April 2026. (5) The VCFTRIAL first-month-free VMware promo is a sunset-in-9-days offer (ends 31 Oct 2026 or after first 50 customers). (6) No dedicated .edu/students free tier and no 'education license' for VPS/dedicated servers was found; the only education-adjacent channels are the qualifying-student quantum notebook program (2024, low visibility now) and the OCRE 2024 GÉANT procurement framework (institutional, negotiated, not free). 7) Startup Program structure is unchanged in substance (€10k Start / up to €100k Scale, 12 months) but currency values differ by country site (US $12k/$120k, CA $14k).

### Paperspace

- **Gradient Free plan — free GPU notebook (M4000) for any new user** (high) — Open free tier on Paperspace Gradient: $0/month, includes 'Basic' free instances — Free GPU (M4000, 8 GB VRAM, 8 vCPU, 30 GB RAM, Maxwell/2015, $0.45/hr if billed) and Free CPU (C4). Docs (verified 13 Jul 2026): free machines can only be used for Notebooks in private workspaces; Free plan includes 5 GB persistent storage. Free-tier auto-shutdown max is 6 hours per the official docs (the pricing page shows 'Auto-shutdown (12 hour limit)'); only 1 free machine runs at a time; free capacity is a shared, non-guaranteed pool (free notebooks can sit Pending in queue). Public projects/notebooks only — no private projects on the Free plan. Upgrade path: Pro $8/mo individual (adds Free P4000/P5000/RTX4000/RTX5000/A4000, 15 GB storage) and Growth $39/mo (adds Free A5000/A6000/A100-80G, 50 GB storage).
  - *Eligibility:* Open to all new users; no .edu email, student status, or startup criteria; no credit card required for the Free plan (official sign-up says 'Create a free account'; card only needed for paid instance usage or paid plans). Not student/startup-specific — a general consumer free tier.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up free at paperspace.com ('Sign up free', or via the Notebooks page 'Sign up with Google/GitHub'), open a private workspace (individual plan), create a Gradient Notebook, choose a pre-built runtime template, select the Free GPU (M4000) machine type, and launch. No payment step.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry or renewal — the Free plan is permanent at $0 while account is active. Overage model, not a trial: storage over $5 GB is billed $0.29/GB/month; running paid instance types bills hourly. Free-plan cap applies; upgrade to Pro/Growth to unlock more free GPU SKUs.
  - *Gotchas:* M4000 is a 2015 Maxwell card — slow for serious training; 8 GB VRAM. Free notebooks are public only (no private projects), 1 running free notebook at a time, capacity is best-effort (queue/Pending possible), session auto-shutdown max 6h per docs (pricing page says 12h — treat as ~half-day sessions). 'Free' is free-tier, not trial-with-expires: paid instances and storage overage are real charges, and the platform has no refunds. No capacity guarantee and free-tier storage is exclusive per region. Free-machine use is limited to Notebooks, not raw Machines/Deployments.
  - *Source:* https://www.paperspace.com/pricing
- **Gradient T0 Team plan — $0 team notebook workspace (10 notebooks)** (medium) — Free team-tier plan: $0 base + utilization on paid instance types. 10 total notebooks, max 1 running at a time, 'Low' instance types only, public notebooks only, 10 GB persistent storage included ($0.29/GB/month overage), 12-hour auto-shutdown limit. Private workspace and team(s) are billed separately.
  - *Eligibility:* Any small team; no education/startup restrictions noted on the pricing page. Free sign-up; card needed only for paid utilization.
  - *How to claim:* Create a free Gradient account, start on the $0 team T0 plan via the pricing page ('Start Now'), invite collaborators to the team to use the 10-notebook allowance.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry/renewal; ongoing $0 base with pay-per-use overage. Cancellable any time from console settings.
  - *Gotchas:* No free GPU entitlement is documented for T0 — the listed 'Low' instance types map to the paid 'Low' tier (C5/C7 CPU, P4000/RTX4000/A4000 GPU), which is card-on-file territory, so T0 mostly gives free CPU/low-tier access; the free M4000 GPU is tied to individual Free/Pro/Growth G-plan workspaces. Overage storage at $0.29/GB adds up; public notebooks only; no refunds on the platform.
  - *Source:* https://www.paperspace.com/pricing
- **DigitalOcean Startups ('Hatch') program — cloud credits, but Paperspace GPUs excluded** (high) — Startups receive DigitalOcean cloud credits for a 12-month period; the amount varies by partner/tier, with a $10,000 monthly credit-usage cap (overage billed), plus 15 months of free Standard support. Credits flow through an approved partner (accelerator/incubator/VC on their partner list) or a direct application via their TypeForm with 'Other' organization. Partner perks add up (e.g. Stripe Atlas 20% off, QuickNode up to $5k, Cloud66 $500). Program now prioritizes AI-native startups; unused credits are forfeited, no extensions.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage company, $10M or less raised total; must have a company website, a matching corporate email, and a registered DigitalOcean team account with a valid credit card. Service-based startups (agencies, consultancies) excluded. Not aimed at students; no .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through an affiliated accelerator/incubator/VC if listed in their partner program, otherwise complete the application on the DigitalOcean Startups page (TypeForm, select 'Other' as affiliated org); on acceptance a 'Welcome Kit' delivers the credits.
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable for 12 months from grant; $10k/month cap; unused credits forfeit at end of period. No renewals.
  - *Gotchas:* Critical for this task: the program FAQ explicitly EXCLUDES Paperspace Machines (and GPU Droplets, H100 GPU Kubernetes, bare-metal GPUs, inference, third-party frontier models) from 'Qualifying Program Services' — GPU credits are a separate, non-guaranteed benefit. So Hatch does NOT reliably buy free Paperspace compute; it credits DigitalOcean core services (Droplets, Spaces, etc.). Card required on the DO team account; no crypto mining; no transfer of credits.
  - *Source:* https://www.digitalocean.com/startups
- **Education license / student program at Paperspace — none found** (medium) — paperspace.com/education returns 404 and the pricing/docs make no mention of any student discount, classroom license, or .edu program as of 2026-10-22. Historically Paperspace's education story was fast.ai course runtimes inside free-tier notebooks; that is a feature, not a funded license.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — no program exists to claim. Students get the same open Free plan as any user (free M4000 GPU notebook, no card, no .edu verification).
  - *How to claim:* n/a
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* Do not treat the general Free plan as an 'education license' — it has the same non-guaranteed free capacity and public-only notebooks for everyone. If an edu program existed it would be a distinct finding; it does not appear to currently.
  - *Source:* https://www.paperspace.com/(404on/education;noeduofferon/pricingordocs.digitalocean.comPaperspacepages)
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes: (1) No Paperspace-specific 2025/2026 program changes found — the public changelog (updates.paperspace.com) shows no 2025-2026 entries on the landing page (last visible 2023-12) and the docs are current as of 2026-07-13 with the Free-tier M4000/GPU+ offering still listed, so the Gradient Free plan appears still live. (2) DigitalOcean no longer markets a standalone 'free trial' page at www.digitalocean.com/free, /trial, or /free-trial (all 404 as of today; homepage has no trial-credits banner), so the classic DO new-account trial credit could not be verified on any official page today — if it still exists it is only discoverable at signup, and I do not list it as a finding. (3) DigitalOcean's Startups/Hatch program has repositioned to prioritize AI-native startups but continues to EXCLUDE Paperspace Machines from qualifying services — so even though the task frames 'startup program' for Paperspace free compute, the only startup-credit vehicle is generic DO credits that do not cover GPU/Paperspace usage. (4) The old Paperspace referral program (free GPU hours for friend referrals, linked in older page footers) has no live page — paperspace.com/referral is 404; treat as sunset. (5) Auto-shutdown for free machines is documented as a 6-hour max in the current DO docs, tighter than the 12-hour figure on the pricing page; treat 6h as the safe operational limit. Nothing on the free M4000 plan was formally sunsetting in the pages read today; it remains the entry free GPU offer. WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so verification relied on direct WebFetch of official pages; unverified-by-search claims are marked medium confidence.

### RunPod Startup Program

- **Startup Program — Starter Tier: $1,000 free credits for pre-Series A startups** (high) — Read runpod.io/startup-program on 2026-10-22. Starter tier grants $1,000 in free credits with full platform access (Pods, Serverless, Clusters, Public Endpoints, Storage), self-serve onboarding, and 'Direct Support' from the Runpod team. Credits have no product-type limitations. Best fit stated as 'Pre-Series A startups with working products and near-term GPU needs.' The program is explicitly selective ('we can't accept every applicant').
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-Series A (earlier-stage) startups with a working product and near-term GPU needs. Page 'strongly prefers' venture backing (Seed–Series B+) with active investors validating traction/runway; pre-funding or bootstrapped applicants are steered to the self-serve platform instead. Must be new to Runpod (program 'primarily designed for new customers onboarding for the first time'), ready to onboard now (not 'in 6 months'), and have infrastructure as a current bottleneck. No .edu requirement, no stated region limit, no credit-card requirement stated on the page, and no published company-age cap (stage language only: 'Pre-Series A').
  - *How to claim:* Click 'Apply now' on https://www.runpod.io/startup-program (self-serve form). Existing Runpod customers below the Growth threshold should contact their account manager or sales@runpod.io; rejected/non-applicant users can simply sign up for pay-as-you-go self-serve without applying.
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiration for Starter credits on the page (only the Growth tier's 12-month agreement is referenced). One-time program; no credit reload mentioned for Starter. Verify expiry in writing when the credits are granted — treat as finite, likely loaded as account credit.
  - *Gotchas:* Not for existing customers unless via account manager. 'Strongly preferred' VC backing means bootstrapped/pre-funding startups may be told to just use self-serve pay-as-you-go instead. Once credits run out it's pure pay-as-you-go with storage/effective costs: volume disks bill ~$0.10/GB/mo running and $0.20/GB/mo idle; stopped (not terminated) pods still bill container disk. Credits are platform-wide but not infinite runway — $1,000 covers e.g. ~300h on an H100 SXM at $3.99/hr.
  - *Source:* https://www.runpod.io/startup-program
- **Startup Program — Growth Tier: $25,000 bonus credits on $50,000 committed spend (credit match, NOT free)** (high) — Depositing up to $50,000 towards a committed contract earns $25,000 in bonus credits ($75,000 total), usable across Pods, Serverless, or Clusters. 12-month agreement with dedicated onboarding call, enterprise-grade uptime/SLA options, and a 'Warm handoff to sales' Enterprise Path. Target: 'Series Seed–B+ startups spending (or ready to spend) $5K–$25K+/mo on GPU infrastructure.' Credits load immediately alongside the deposit; bonus is one-time, explicitly 'no credit reload.'
  - *Eligibility:* VC-backed Seed–B+ startups, new to Runpod, ready to onboard now, 'pre prepared to invest upfront for credit match,' open to enterprise conversation. No .edu or region requirements stated.
  - *How to claim:* Same 'Apply now' CTA on https://www.runpod.io/startup-program; existing customers contact their account manager or sales@runpod.io.
  - *Expiry:* Credits loaded under a 12-month agreement; one-time bonus with no reload/renewal. No separate renewal track published.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free caveat: this is a 50% credit match on $50k committed spend, not a free offer — do not count it as free compute. Commitment is upfront and contracted. One-time only.
  - *Source:* https://www.runpod.io/startup-program
- **No public student/.edu free offer — Academic Research program is institutional and sales-led** (high) — The pricing page (read 2026-10-22) shows no free tier, sign-up bonus, trial credits, or student discount anywhere. The Academic Research program targets universities, departments, and labs: lab/department-level projects prioritized, individual students/solo researchers only 'on a case-by-case basis,' access via institutional enrollment with university SSO integration, billing via custom invoicing aligned to academic calendars/grants/shared lab accounts, and manual application review with follow-up in 7-10 business days then a discovery call. No free GPU hours, no dollar-amount education discount, and no .edu sign-up-and-go tier is published.
  - *Eligibility:* Institutions (university SSO, grant/department funding) rather than individuals. No .edu free allocation; individuals only on a case-by-case basis.
  - *How to claim:* Contact form or phone +1 (888) 478-6012 via https://www.runpod.io/academic-research ('Get in touch' / 'Request a demo').
  - *Expiry:* N/A — no published free/discounted terms to expire.
  - *Gotchas:* If the goal is free student compute, RunPod is a weak option versus Kaggle (free P100 notebook quota), HF Spaces, or Modal's up-to-$10k academic credits. Any education discount is negotiated, not self-serve; expect no public number.
  - *Source:* https://www.runpod.io/academic-research
- **Partner/event-sponsored free compute (secondary, non-self-serve offers)** (medium) — Several one-off free-compute offers around RunPod exist but are not standing self-serve programs: (1) Santa Cruz Accelerates 2025 cohort via Santa Cruz Works — eligible accelerator founders can apply for three tracks: up to 1,000 free H100 GPU hours, up to 1,000,000 free Serverless requests, or up to 750 free multi-node H100 compute hours (partner announcement, Jul 31, 2025). (2) $100 free GPU credits for ARC-AGI-2 participants (RunPod LinkedIn post, Mar 2025, application form, event-limited). (3) TreeHacks 2026 (Stanford, Oct 2026): RunPod donated $20k+ in credits to hackathon teams — sponsorship, not claimable by the public. Hackathons/events like these recur but each is separate and time-boxed.
  - *Eligibility:* Santa Cruz: founders enrolled in the Santa Cruz Accelerates 2025 cohort (apply through Santa Cruz Works or mention them on the referral form). ARC-AGI-2: participants of that specific challenge. TreeHacks: hackathon teams only.
  - *How to claim:* Santa Cruz: apply through Santa Cruz Works (santacruzworks.org) or the RunPod referral form mentioning Santa Cruz Works. ARC-AGI-2: via the event application link. General: watch runpod.io/blog for current sponsorships.
  - *Expiry:* All time-boxed/cohort-specific (2025 cohort, 2025 challenge, 2026 event). Not renewable; likely expired or past for 2025 items as of 2026-10-22 unless re-run.
  - *Gotchas:* These do not constitute a recurring student/startup free tier; amounts are per-cohort/per-event and require the external partner's program eligibility, not just a RunPod account.
  - *Source:* https://www.santacruzworks.org/news/run-pod-santa-cruz-works-accelerator
- **2025–26 change:** No 2025-2026 sunset or material change of the RunPod Startup Program was found: the official page was live and verified on 2026-10-22 with the Starter $1,000 / Growth $25,000-match tiers matching prior public claims. No public sign-up bonus credits for regular users ever appeared on the pricing page, so there is nothing in that lane to sunset. Event-based free offers ($100 ARC-AGI-2 credits, Mar 2025; Santa Cruz Accelerates 2025 cohort; TreeHacks 2026 sponsorship) are time-boxed and effectively closed by 2026-10-22 unless re-announced. The second web-search on change-tracking hit the session search budget and did not complete, so pre-2026 history of the startup program's tiers is not independently cross-checked beyond the official page; re-verify tier amounts on the official page before relying on them.

### SGLang

- **SGLang open-source inference engine (Apache-2.0) — permanently free** (high) — SGLang is a fully open-source LLM/VLM/diffusion serving engine (RadixAttention prefix caching, structured generation, speculative decoding) under Apache-2.0: $0 license, no subscription, no per-token/seat fees, unlimited usage, indefinite. 'Free' here means the software; you still pay for your own GPU compute (e.g., ~$3.39/hr median on-demand H100 as of Sep 2026). The commercial backer RadixArk (launched May 2026, $100M seed at $400M valuation) sells managed services, but the open engine remains free. No free tier, trial, education license, or startup program exists on SGLang's official site (verified sglang.io 2026-10-22: no pricing page, no student/startup program; only 'enterprise adoption/consulting/sponsorship' contact at sglang@lmsys.org).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, anywhere — no .edu email, no startup age/funding cap, no credit card, no region limits. Open-source license is unconditional (Apache-2.0). Students get the identical free software as enterprises.
  - *How to claim:* git clone or pip install/uv sglang (or Docker image) from https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang; run on any supported GPU/TPU/NPU/CPU (A100/H100/H200/B200/MI300X/TPU v6e-v7/Apple Silicon etc.). No account, no signup, no key.
  - *Expiry:* Permanent (open-source license; no expiry). Ecosystem sub-projects (SGLang Diffusion, SGLang-Omni, SpecForge, HiCache/Mooncake) are in the same repo/organization and open-sourced too.
  - *Gotchas:* The $0 software does NOT cover compute: GPU rental is the real cost (on-demand H100 ~$3.39/hr; below ~2.5-3B tokens/mo a per-token API is usually cheaper). Model weights for served models come from other labs — some models (e.g., certain gated LLMs) require their own license acceptance. Third-party 'pricing' sites (costbench, aiindigo, aitoolsatlas) describe a '$0 free tier' but aitoolsatlas's 'education pricing available' claim is generic boilerplate, not a real SGLang program. The 'median contract $51,500/yr' figure on costbench refers to RadixArk commercial managed deals, not the free engine.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang
- **AMD Developer Cloud free MI300X credits (~$100 / ~50 hrs) via SGLang partner guide** (medium) — AMD Developer Cloud has offered free MI300X GPU access to developers, and SGLang's own project posted (2026-03-17) a guide to run a full stack (Qwen3.5-122B-A10B-FP8 served via SGLang in one Docker command, selectable in OpenClaw's onboarding CLI) 'at zero cost' on a single MI300X, quantified as ~$100 AMD Developer Cloud credits (~50 hrs of MI300X). AMD replied to the post confirming the guide. This is partner-sourced free compute, not an SGLang program.
  - *Eligibility:* AMD Developer Cloud account holders (developer sign-up); guide is aimed at any developer/student/startup building an agent stack — no .edu requirement stated, no funding cap. US-based cloud (AMD data centers); account verification typically required.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at devcloud.amd.com, apply for the free MI300X trial/credits, then follow SGLang's published guide (shared 2026-03-17 on the SGLang LinkedIn, shortened link lnkd.in/g2UZFehh) to deploy Qwen3.5-122B-A10B-FP8 via SGLang in one Docker command.
  - *Expiry:* Credits are promotional and time-boxed; ~50 hrs / $100 figure is from the Mar 2026 post — exact expiry window not confirmed (AMD's pages are JS-only and I could not read official terms today; search budget exhausted).
  - *Gotchas:* Not an SGLang-owned program — terms/limits/credits can change or be revoked by AMD; capacity on MI300X instances is limited and often queued; data egress/model-download costs may apply; 'free' applies only while credits last, after which MI300X time is metered. Verify current credit amounts at devcloud.amd.com before relying on this.
  - *Source:* https://www.linkedin.com/posts/sgl-project_openclaw-on-amd-developer-cloud-free-deployment-activity-7439770026288594944-0tOc
- **2025–26 change:** No SGLang-specific free offer was sunset or materially changed in 2025-2026: there is no SGLang free tier, trial credit, startup program, or education license to sunset — the 'free offer' is the Apache-2.0 software itself, which is permanent. Material 2025-2026 context: (1) the commercial entity RadixArk launched May 2026 ($100M seed, $400M valuation) with managed-inference/managed-training services on top of the open core — at this date it has announced no startup or education program, so nothing free exists on that front to claim; (2) SGLang joined the PyTorch ecosystem during 2025; (3) a free-compute path via partner AMD Developer Cloud (MI300X, ~$100/~50 hrs) was highlighted by the SGLang project in March 2026, but AMD's official terms could not be read (JS-only pages) and web-search budget was exhausted, so treat that figure as unverified current. Third-party discount sites claiming SGLang 'education pricing' (e.g., aitoolsatlas.ai) are auto-generated generic text, not a real program.

### Thunder Compute

- **Student Credits — $20 free credit (A6000/L40 only)** (high) — Official /students page (read 2026-10-22): sign up with your student email to get $20 of free Thunder Compute credit, added automatically after signup ('no application and no waiting'). Credit is restricted to RTX A6000 and L40 GPUs — at listed on-demand rates (A6000 $0.35/hr, L40 $0.79/hr) that is ~57 A6000-hours or ~25 L40-hours. Not usable on H100 SXM ($3.20/hr) or A100 80GB ($1.09/hr).
  - *Eligibility:* Students enrolled at US universities only ('Only students enrolled at US universities qualify'). Must sign up with a university email; the system 'verify your email domain automatically'. No other credential (not even strictly .edu, though the company's own blog FAQ phrases it as 'verified .edu email') is stated. No credit card or payment method is mentioned as required to claim the credit; after it is exhausted, standard pay-as-you-go billing applies.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at console.thundercompute.com/signup using your university email; 2) email domain is verified automatically and the $20 credit is added to the account right after signup; 3) use the credit on A6000 or L40 instances. No application, no waiting.
  - *Expiry:* No expiration date, renewal cycle, or re-eligibility window is stated on the /students page. After the credit is spent, usage continues at normal pay-as-you-go rates.
  - *Gotchas:* US-institution-only; credit locked to A6000/L40 (cheapest GPUs in their lineup, not the H100/A100 most workloads need); fraud or abuse 'may result in credit removal or account suspension'. One account/student implied by the auto-verification; no stated way to re-claim. WebSearch was unavailable this session (budget exhausted), so a 2025-2026 history of the offer (e.g., whether it was ever larger or previously also covered A100) could not be verified.
  - *Source:* https://thundercompute.com/students
- **Referral Program — 3% of referred customers' lifetime spend, paid in credits** (high) — Official /referrals page (read 2026-10-22): 'Earn 3% in credits on the lifetime spend of every customer you refer to Thunder Compute.' Example given: refer $10,000 in spend -> $300 in credits. Recurring/ongoing, not one-time. Referring 25 users who spend money unlocks affiliate status. No dollar cap or per-referral limit is stated beyond the illustrative example.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to any Thunder Compute user (no student/startup-specific tier, region or startup-age criterion stated). Payout is Thunder Compute credits, not cash.
  - *How to claim:* Share your referral link; each referred paying customer generates 3% of their lifetime spend as credit to your account.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry stated; credit accrues for the lifetime of referred customers' spend.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits only, not cash. 'Fraud or abuse of the referral program may result in credit removal and/or account suspension.' You need referrals who actually spend; a referred free-credit-only student account generates no referral value unless they top up. Not a startup program — it's a generic growth incentive.
  - *Source:* https://thundercompute.com/referrals
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22 Thunder Compute has no free tier, no trial credits, and no startup program: /startup and /changelog both 404; the pricing page lists only paid on-demand rates (A6000 $0.35/hr, L40 $0.79/hr, A100 80GB $1.09/hr, H100 SXM $3.20/hr; sandbox GPUs H100 $2.95/GPU-hr and A100 $1.95/GPU-hr; data egress $0.00/GB; disk $0.219/GB/mo) with a reserved/volume-pricing mention that is discounted committed spend, not free; the sign-up page (console.thundercompute.com/signup) mentions no promotional credit. The company's own 'Free Cloud GPU Credits in 2026' blog directory attributes to Thunder Compute only the $20 student credit and the 3% referral program — no startup-tier entry. So there is no evidence a startup program ever existed or was sunset; the only distinct free offers are the student credit and the referral credits above. Caveat: WebSearch's session budget was exhausted, so 2025-2026 change history was verified by reading official pages only, not by news search.
